````math
``` math
\forall g,h\in\mathbb{R}^{Y}:\varphi_{x}^{*}(g)-\varphi_{x}^{*}(h)-\langle\nabla\varphi_{x}^{*}(h),g-h\rangle\geq 0\,,
``` |  | (20) |

where $`\nabla\varphi_{x}^{*}(g)`$ is the unique maximizer in the
definition of $`\varphi_{x}^{*}(g)`$.

Next, we relate $`V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\mu)`$ to
$`\varphi_{x}^{*}`$. For any $`\mu,\pi`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu)=\mathcal{P}(\mu\succ\pi)+\lambda\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\widetilde{\pi})=\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\langle{\bf P}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{x}\mu(x),\pi(x)\rangle+\lambda\KL(\pi(x)\|\widetilde{\pi}(x))}}\right]\,,
``` |  |

Thus, using the fact that optimization is performed over all functions
from $`\mathcal{X}`$ to $`\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle V_{\lambda}^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}(\mu)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\min_{\pi\in\Pi}V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu)=-\sup_{\pi\in\Pi}\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\langle-{\bf P}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{x}\mu(x),\pi(x)\rangle-\lambda\KL(\pi(x)\|\widetilde{\pi}(x))}}\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sup_{q\in\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}}\langle-{\bf P}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{x}\mu(x),q\rangle-\lambda\KL(q\|\widetilde{\pi}(x))}}\right]=-\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\varphi_{x}^{*}(-{\bf P}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{x}\mu(x))}}\right]\,.`$ |  |

Also, for any $`x\in\mathrm{supp}(\rho)`$, the unique minimizer of
$`V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\cdot,\mu)`$ at context $`x`$ is given
by

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
[\nu^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\mu)](x)=\argmin_{q\in\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}}\langle{\bf P}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{x}\mu(x),q\rangle+\lambda\KL(q\|\widetilde{\pi}(x))=\nabla\varphi_{x}^{*}(-{\bf P}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{x}\mu(x))\,.
``` |  |

Using these relations, we can now prove the smoothness property of
$`V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}`$. Applying
([20](#A2.E20 "In Proof. ‣ B.2 Regularized Preference and Value ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
pointwise at each $`x\in\mathrm{supp}(\rho)`$ with
$`g=-{\bf P}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{x}\mu(x)`$,
$`h=-{\bf P}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{x}\mu^{\prime}(x)`$, we
get

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\varphi_{x}^{*}(-{\bf P}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{x}\mu(x))-\varphi_{x}^{*}(-{\bf P}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{x}\mu^{\prime}(x))-\langle\nabla\varphi_{x}^{*}(-{\bf P}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{x}\mu^{\prime}(x)),-{\bf P}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{x}(\mu(x)-\mu^{\prime}(x))\rangle\geq 0\,.`$ |  |

Taking expectation over $`x\sim\rho`$ and multiplying by $`-1`$, we
obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[-\varphi_{x}^{*}(-{\bf P}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{x}\mu(x))}}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[-\varphi_{x}^{*}(-{\bf P}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{x}\mu^{\prime}(x))}}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\langle\nabla\varphi_{x}^{*}(-{\bf P}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{x}\mu^{\prime}(x)),{\bf P}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{x}(\mu(x)-\mu^{\prime}(x))\rangle}}\right]\leq 0\,.`$ |  |

Rewriting using the relations to $`V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}`$
and $`\nu^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}`$, we get

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\mu)-V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\mu^{\prime})-\mathcal{P}(\mu-\mu^{\prime}\succ\nu^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\mu^{\prime}))\leq 0\,.`$ |  |

∎

###### Lemma 6 (Suboptimality decomposition).

Let $`\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}`$ be a solution to
([17](#A2.E17 "In B.2 Regularized Preference and Value ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")).
Then, for any policies $`\pi,\mu\in\Pi`$, it holds

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu)\leq\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda})+\frac{1}{2}\,\|\pi-\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}\|_{1,\rho}\cdot\|\mu-\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}\|_{1,\rho}\,.
``` |  |

###### Proof.

By definition,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}-\mathcal{P}(\pi\succ\mu)+\lambda\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\widetilde{\pi})-\lambda\KL_{\rho}(\mu\|\widetilde{\pi})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}-\mathcal{P}(\pi\succ\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda})+\lambda\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\widetilde{\pi})-\lambda\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}\|\widetilde{\pi})}_{\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda})}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}-\mathcal{P}(\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}\succ\mu)+\lambda\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}\|\widetilde{\pi})-\lambda\KL_{\rho}(\mu\|\widetilde{\pi})}_{\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda},\mu)}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{P}(\pi-\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}\succ\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}-\mu)\,,`$ |  |

where, by
bilinearity ([15](#A2.E15 "In B.1 Preference Properties ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
and symmetry, we used

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{P}(\pi-\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}\succ\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}-\mu)=\mathcal{P}(\pi\succ\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda})-\mathcal{P}(\pi\succ\mu)-\mathcal{P}(\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}\succ\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda})+\mathcal{P}(\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}\succ\mu),
``` |  |

and
$`\mathcal{P}(\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}\succ\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda})=1/2`$.

Since $`\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}`$ is a Nash equilibrium of
the regularized game, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{P}^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}\succ\mu)\geq\frac{1}{2}
``` |  |

for all $`\mu`$, hence
$`\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda},\mu)\leq 0`$.
It remains to bound the last term. For each $`x`$, both
$`\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(x)`$ and $`\mu(x)`$ are
distributions, so
$`\sum_{y}(\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(y|x)-\mu(y|x))=0`$.
Therefore, by
Lemma [2](#Thmlemma2 "Lemma 2 (Lipschitz property of preferences). ‣ B.1 Preference Properties ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
applied to $`u(x)=\pi(x)-\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(x)`$,
$`v(x)=\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(x)-\mu(x)`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\bigl|\mathcal{P}(\pi-\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}\succ\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}-\mu)\bigr|\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\pi-\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}\|_{1,\rho}\cdot\|\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}-\mu\|_{1,\rho}\,.
``` |  |

Combining the above inequalities yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu)\leq\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda})+\frac{1}{2}\|\pi-\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}\|_{1,\rho}\cdot\|\mu-\pi^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}\|_{1,\rho}\,.
``` |  |

∎

### B.3 Proximal Point Method

We now specialize to the $`\beta`$-regularized contextual game with
respect to $`\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}\in\Pi`$:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\min_{\mu\in\Pi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(\pi\succ\mu)\triangleq\mathcal{P}(\pi\succ\mu)-\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})+\beta\KL_{\rho}(\mu\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})}}\right\}\,.
``` |  |

We define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{\beta}(\pi,\mu)\triangleq\mathcal{P}(\mu\succ\pi)+\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})\,,\qquad V^{\star}_{\beta}(\mu)\triangleq\min_{\pi\in\Pi}V_{\beta}(\pi,\mu)\,,
``` |  |

and let $`\pi^{\star}_{\beta}`$ denote the $`\beta`$-regularized von
Neumann winner in this contextual game. The suboptimality is

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt_{\beta}(\pi,\mu)\triangleq\tfrac{1}{2}-\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(\pi\succ\mu),\qquad\subopt_{\beta}(\pi)\triangleq\max_{\mu}\subopt_{\beta}(\pi,\mu).
``` |  |

##### Method description.

The iterates of the approximate proximal point (PP) method can be
written as follows:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\pi_{t+1}\approx\argmax_{\pi\in\Pi}\min_{\mu\in\Pi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(\pi\succ\mu)-\frac{\beta}{\eta}\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi_{t})+\frac{\beta}{\eta}\KL_{\rho}(\mu\|\pi_{t})}}\right\}\,,
``` |  | (21) |

where $`\eta>0`$ is a PP learning rate. To define the success criteria
for approximation, we introduce the regularized value

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{t}(\pi,\mu)\triangleq V_{\beta}(\pi,\mu)+\frac{\beta}{\eta}\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi_{t})\,,\qquad V^{\star}_{t}(\mu)\triangleq\min_{\pi\in\Pi}V_{t}(\pi,\mu)\,.
``` |  |

For the exact solution to
([21](#A2.E21 "In Method description. ‣ B.3 Proximal Point Method ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")),
the updated policy $`\pi_{t+1}`$ is its own best response, i.e.,
$`\nabla_{\pi}V_{t}(\pi_{t+1},\pi_{t+1})(x)\propto{\bf 1}`$ for all
$`x\in\mathcal{X}`$. In the contextual setting, we measure the
approximation error by

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\big\|\nabla_{\pi}V_{t}(\pi_{t+1},\pi_{t+1})\big\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\Big[\big\|\nabla_{\pi}V_{t}(\pi_{t+1},\pi_{t+1})(x)\big\|_{\mathrm{sp}}^{2}\Big].
``` |  |

We say that $`\pi_{t+1}`$ is an $`\varepsilon`$-approximation of
([21](#A2.E21 "In Method description. ‣ B.3 Proximal Point Method ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
if

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\big\|\nabla_{\pi}V_{t}(\pi_{t+1},\pi_{t+1})\big\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\leq\varepsilon.
``` |  |

For brevity, we denote
$`\zeta_{t+1}\triangleq\nabla_{\pi}V_{t}(\pi_{t+1},\pi_{t+1})`$.

##### Equivalence with a mixed-reference value.

For each $`x\in\mathcal{X}`$, consider the regularization term

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\beta\KL(\pi(x)\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(x))+\frac{\beta}{\eta}\KL(\pi(x)\|\pi_{t}(x)).
``` |  |

A direct calculation shows that there exists a policy
$`\tilde{\pi}_{t}`$ such that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\tilde{\pi}_{t}(y\mid x)\propto[\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(y\mid x)]^{\eta/(1+\eta)}[\pi_{t}(y\mid x)]^{1/(1+\eta)},
``` |  |

and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\beta\KL(\pi(x)\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(x))+\frac{\beta}{\eta}\KL(\pi(x)\|\pi_{t}(x))=\beta\Big(1+\frac{1}{\eta}\Big)\KL(\pi(x)\|\tilde{\pi}_{t}(x))+C_{t}(x),
``` |  |

where $`C_{t}(x)`$ is independent of $`\pi(x)`$. Averaging over
$`x\sim\rho`$, we obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{t}(\pi,\mu)=\mathcal{P}(\mu\succ\pi)+\beta\Big(1+\frac{1}{\eta}\Big)\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\tilde{\pi}_{t})+\text{const}(\pi^{\mathrm{ref}},\pi_{t}).
``` |  |

Hence, by
Lemma [4](#Thmlemma4 "Lemma 4 (Strong convexity in 𝜋 with respect to KL_𝜌). ‣ B.2 Regularized Preference and Value ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with $`\lambda=\beta(1+1/\eta)`$ and reference $`\tilde{\pi}_{t}`$, we
have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{t}(\pi,\mu)-V_{t}(\pi^{\prime},\mu)=\langle\nabla_{\pi}V_{t}(\pi^{\prime},\mu),\pi-\pi^{\prime}\rangle_{\rho}+\beta\Big(1+\frac{1}{\eta}\Big)\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi^{\prime})
``` |  | (22) |

for all $`\pi,\pi^{\prime},\mu\in\Pi`$.

###### Proposition 2 (Convergence of approximate PP method).

Assume that each iterate $`\pi_{t+1}`$ is an
$`\varepsilon`$-approximation in the sense that
$`\big\|\nabla_{\pi}V_{t}(\pi_{t+1},\pi_{t+1})\big\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\leq\varepsilon`$
for all $`t\geq 0`$. Then, for any $`t\in\mathbb{N}`$, it holds

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|\pi_{t})\leq(1+\eta/2)^{-t}\cdot\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|\pi_{0})+\frac{2\varepsilon}{\beta^{2}}\,,
``` |  |

and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{t})\leq(1+\eta/2)^{-t}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\beta}+\frac{2\beta}{\eta}+3}}\right)\cdot\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|\pi_{0})+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2+\frac{2}{\beta^{3}}+\frac{4}{\eta\beta}+\frac{6}{\beta^{2}}}}\right)\varepsilon\,,
``` |  |

and, moreover,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\big\|\log\pi_{t}-\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\big\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\leq(1+\eta)^{-t}\big\|\log\pi_{0}-\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\big\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}+\frac{2}{\beta^{2}}\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|\pi_{0})\cdot(1+\eta/2)^{-t}+\frac{2(1+\beta^{2})\cdot\varepsilon}{\beta^{4}}\,.
``` |  |

###### Remark 1 (On the choice of $`\eta`$).

The present bound suggests that taking $`\eta\to+\infty`$ is the best
solution since it will drive convergence in just one iterate. However,
the actual tradeoff is implicitly hidden in the value of
$`\varepsilon`$: the speed of convergence of the internal subproblems
depends on $`\eta`$ and a smaller $`\eta`$ makes these subproblems
better-conditioned thanks to stronger regularization. In particular, the
convergence of Mirror Prox-style methods requires
$`\eta=\mathcal{O}(\beta)`$ if transferred to our setting ([Cen et al.,
2024](#bib.bib14)).

###### Proof.

We split the proof into three parts: convergence in $`\KL_{\rho}`$,
convergence in suboptimality, and convergence in the span seminorm of
log-probabilities.

##### Convergence in $`\KL_{\rho}`$.

Define
$`A_{t}\triangleq\sqrt{\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|\pi_{t})}`$.
Applying ([22](#A2.E22 "In Equivalence with a mixed-reference value. ‣ B.3 Proximal Point Method ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
with $`\pi=\pi^{\star}_{\beta}`$, $`\pi^{\prime}=\pi_{t+1}`$,
$`\mu=\pi_{t+1}`$, we obtain

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\beta\Big(1+\frac{1}{\eta}\Big)\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|\pi_{t+1})`$ | $`\displaystyle=V_{t}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta},\pi_{t+1})-V_{t}(\pi_{t+1},\pi_{t+1})+\langle\nabla_{\pi}V_{t}(\pi_{t+1},\pi_{t+1}),\pi_{t+1}-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\rangle_{\rho}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=V_{\beta}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta},\pi_{t+1})+\frac{\beta}{\eta}\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|\pi_{t})-V_{\beta}(\pi_{t+1},\pi_{t+1})-\frac{\beta}{\eta}\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{t+1}\|\pi_{t})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad+\langle\zeta_{t+1},\pi_{t+1}-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\rangle_{\rho}.`$ |  |

Using the definition of $`V_{\beta}`$ and $`\mathcal{P}_{\beta}`$, we
have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{\beta}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta},\pi_{t+1})-V_{\beta}(\pi_{t+1},\pi_{t+1})=\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(\pi_{t+1}\succ\pi^{\star}_{\beta})-\frac{1}{2}=-\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{t+1},\pi^{\star}_{\beta})\leq 0.
``` |  |

Dropping the non-negative term
$`\frac{\beta}{\eta}\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{t+1}\|\pi_{t})`$, we get

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\beta\Big(1+\frac{1}{\eta}\Big)A_{t+1}^{2}\leq\frac{\beta}{\eta}A_{t}^{2}+\langle\zeta_{t+1},\pi_{t+1}-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\rangle_{\rho}-\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{t+1},\pi^{\star}_{\beta})\,,
``` |  | (23) |

where we can drop the last term too since it is non-positive. For each
$`x`$, since $`\sum_{y}(\pi_{t+1}(y|x)-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y|x))=0`$, we
have for any scalar function $`c(x)`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\langle\zeta_{t+1}(x),\pi_{t+1}(x)-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(x)\rangle=\langle\zeta_{t+1}(x)-c(x)\cdot{\bf 1},\pi_{t+1}(x)-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(x)\rangle.
``` |  |

Thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\langle\zeta_{t+1},\pi_{t+1}-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\rangle_{\rho}\leq\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\bigl[\|\zeta_{t+1}(x)-c(x)\cdot{\bf 1}\|_{\infty}\|\pi_{t+1}(x)-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(x)\|_{1}\bigr].
``` |  |

Using Cauchy–Schwarz,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\langle\zeta_{t+1},\pi_{t+1}-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\rangle_{\rho}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\bigl[\|\zeta_{t+1}(x)-c(x)\cdot{\bf 1}\|_{\infty}\|\pi_{t+1}(x)-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(x)\|_{1}\bigr]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\zeta_{t+1}(x)-c(x)\cdot{\bf 1}\|_{\infty}^{2}}}\right]\cdot\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\pi_{t+1}(x)-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(x)\|_{1}^{2}}}\right]}\,.`$ |  |

Finally, minimizing over $`c(x)`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\langle\zeta_{t+1},\pi_{t+1}-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\rangle_{\rho}\leq\lVert\zeta_{t+1}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}\cdot\lVert\pi_{t+1}-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\rVert_{1,\rho}\,.
``` |  |

By Pinsker’s
inequality ([16](#A2.E16 "In B.1 Preference Properties ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")),

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\pi_{t+1}-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|_{1,\rho}\leq\sqrt{2\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|\pi_{t+1})}=\sqrt{2}\,A_{t+1},
``` |  |

hence

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\langle\zeta_{t+1},\pi_{t+1}-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\rangle_{\rho}\leq\sqrt{2}\,\|\zeta_{t+1}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}A_{t+1}.
``` |  |

Substituting
into ([23](#A2.E23 "In Convergence in KL_𝜌. ‣ B.3 Proximal Point Method ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")),
we obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\beta\Big(1+\frac{1}{\eta}\Big)A_{t+1}^{2}\leq\frac{\beta}{\eta}A_{t}^{2}+\sqrt{2}\,\|\zeta_{t+1}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}A_{t+1}.
``` |  |

Dividing by $`\beta(1+1/\eta)`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
A_{t+1}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{1+\eta}A_{t}^{2}+2\cdot\frac{\eta\sqrt{2\lVert\zeta_{t+1}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}}}{2\beta\cdot(1+\eta)}\cdot A_{t+1}\,.
``` |  |

Solving this quadratic inequality in $`A_{t+1}`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
A_{t+1}\leq\frac{\eta\sqrt{2\lVert\zeta_{t+1}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}}}{2\beta\cdot(1+\eta)}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{1+\eta}A_{t}^{2}+\frac{\eta^{2}\lVert\zeta_{t+1}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}}{2\beta^{2}\cdot(1+\eta)^{2}}}\,.
``` |  |

Taking the square of both sides and using an inequality
$`(a+b)^{2}\leq(1+\alpha)a^{2}+(1+1/\alpha)b^{2}`$ for any $`\alpha>0`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
A_{t+1}^{2}\leq\frac{1+\alpha}{1+\eta}A_{t}^{2}+(2+\alpha+1/\alpha)\frac{\eta^{2}\lVert\zeta_{t+1}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}}{2\beta^{2}\cdot(1+\eta)^{2}}\,.
``` |  |

Taking $`\alpha`$ as a solution to $`(1+\alpha)/(1+\eta)=1/(1+\eta/2)`$,
we have $`2+\alpha+1/\alpha=(1+\eta)^{2}/(\eta/2\cdot(1+\eta/2))`$, thus
we achieve

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
A_{t+1}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{1+\eta/2}A_{t}^{2}+\frac{\eta\lVert\zeta_{t+1}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}}{\beta^{2}\cdot(1+\eta/2)}\leq\frac{1}{1+\eta/2}A_{t}^{2}+\frac{\eta\cdot\varepsilon}{\beta^{2}\cdot(1+\eta/2)}\,,
``` |  |

where the condition on a gradient error
$`\lVert\zeta_{t+1}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\leq\varepsilon`$ is
applied. Unrolling this inequality, we achieve for any
$`T\in\mathbb{N}`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
A_{T}^{2}\leq\frac{A_{0}^{2}}{(1+\eta/2)^{T}}+\frac{\eta\cdot\varepsilon}{\beta^{2}(1+\eta/2)}\cdot\frac{1}{1-1/(1+\eta/2)}\leq\frac{A_{0}^{2}}{(1+\eta/2)^{T}}+\frac{2\varepsilon}{\beta^{2}}\,.
``` |  |

##### Convergence in suboptimality.

Rearranging ([23](#A2.E23 "In Convergence in KL_𝜌. ‣ B.3 Proximal Point Method ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")),
we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{t+1},\pi^{\star}_{\beta})\leq(\beta/\eta)A_{t}^{2}+\sqrt{2}\|\zeta_{t+1}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}\cdot A_{t+1}\,.
``` |  |

Let $`\mu^{\star}_{t+1}\in\argmax_{\mu}\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{t+1},\mu)`$
be a worst-case opponent so that
$`\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{t+1})=\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{t+1},\mu^{\star}_{t+1})`$.
Applying
Lemma [6](#Thmlemma6 "Lemma 6 (Suboptimality decomposition). ‣ B.2 Regularized Preference and Value ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with $`\pi^{\pi^{\mathrm{ref}},\star}_{\lambda}=\pi^{\star}_{\beta}`$
and $`\lambda=\beta,\widetilde{\pi}=\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}`$ and triangle
inequality, we get

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{t+1})`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq(\beta/\eta)A_{t}^{2}+\sqrt{2}\|\zeta_{t+1}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}\cdot A_{t+1}+\frac{1}{2}\|\pi_{t+1}-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|_{1,\rho}\cdot\|\mu^{\star}_{t+1}-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|_{1,\rho}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq(\beta/\eta)A_{t}^{2}+\sqrt{2}\|\zeta_{t+1}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}\cdot A_{t+1}+\frac{1}{2}\|\pi_{t+1}-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|_{1,\rho}\cdot\|\mu^{\star}_{t+1}-\pi_{t+1}\|_{1,\rho}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\lVert\pi_{t+1}-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\rVert_{1,\rho}^{2}\,.`$ |  |

By Pinsker’s
inequality ([16](#A2.E16 "In B.1 Preference Properties ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")),

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\pi_{t+1}-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|_{1,\rho}\leq\sqrt{2\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|\pi_{t+1})}=\sqrt{2}\cdot A_{t+1}.
``` |  |

Next, we show that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{t+1}\|\mu^{\star}_{t+1})\leq\frac{1}{\beta}\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{t+1}).
``` |  |

For any $`\pi,\mu`$, a direct calculation gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{\beta}(\pi,\pi)-V_{\beta}(\mu,\pi)=\mathcal{P}(\pi\succ\pi)+\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})-\mathcal{P}(\pi\succ\mu)-\beta\KL_{\rho}(\mu\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})=\subopt_{\beta}(\pi,\mu).
``` |  |

Therefore, for fixed $`\pi`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt_{\beta}(\pi)=\max_{\mu}\subopt_{\beta}(\pi,\mu)=V_{\beta}(\pi,\pi)-\min_{\mu}V_{\beta}(\mu,\pi).
``` |  |

This implies that $`\mu^{\star}_{t+1}`$ is a minimizer of
$`V_{\beta}(\cdot,\pi_{t+1})`$ in its first argument. Applying
Lemma [4](#Thmlemma4 "Lemma 4 (Strong convexity in 𝜋 with respect to KL_𝜌). ‣ B.2 Regularized Preference and Value ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with $`\lambda=\beta`$, $`\mu=\pi_{t+1}`$, $`\pi=\pi_{t+1}`$, and
$`\pi^{\prime}=\mu^{\star}_{t+1}`$, we obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{\beta}(\pi_{t+1},\pi_{t+1})-V_{\beta}(\mu^{\star}_{t+1},\pi_{t+1})\geq\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{t+1}\|\mu^{\star}_{t+1}).
``` |  |

The left-hand side is precisely $`\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{t+1})`$, hence

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{t+1}\|\mu^{\star}_{t+1})\leq\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{t+1}).
``` |  |

Using Pinsker’s inequality once more,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\pi_{t+1}-\mu^{\star}_{t+1}\|_{1,\rho}\leq\sqrt{2\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{t+1}\|\mu^{\star}_{t+1})}\leq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta}\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{t+1})}.
``` |  |

Combining the two bounds, we get

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{1}{2}\|\pi_{t+1}-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|_{1,\rho}\cdot\|\pi_{t+1}-\mu^{\star}_{t+1}\|_{1,\rho}\leq\sqrt{1/\beta}\,A_{t+1}\sqrt{\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{t+1})}.
``` |  |

Denoting $`B_{t+1}=\sqrt{\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{t+1})}`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
B_{t+1}^{2}\leq A_{t+1}^{2}+(\beta/\eta)A_{t}^{2}+\sqrt{2}\|\zeta_{t+1}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}A_{t+1}+2\sqrt{1/(4\beta)}A_{t+1}B_{t+1}\,.
``` |  |

Solving this quadratic, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
B_{t+1}\leq\sqrt{1/(4\beta)}\cdot A_{t+1}+\sqrt{(1+1/(4\beta))\cdot A_{t+1}^{2}+(\beta/\eta)A_{t}^{2}+\sqrt{2\cdot\lVert\zeta_{t+1}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}}\cdot A_{t+1}}\,,
``` |  |

and, after taking square and using an inequality
$`(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}`$ and $`2ab\leq a^{2}+b^{2}`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
B_{t+1}^{2}\leq(2+1/\beta)A_{t+1}^{2}+2(\beta/\eta)A_{t}^{2}+2\sqrt{2\lVert\zeta_{t+1}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}}\cdot A_{t+1}\leq(1/\beta+3)A_{t+1}^{2}+2(\beta/\eta)A_{t}^{2}+2\lVert\zeta_{t+1}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\,.
``` |  |

After plugging in the bound on $`A_{t+1}^{2}`$, $`A_{t}^{2}`$, and
$`\lVert\zeta_{t+1}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}`$ we conclude the
statement.

##### Convergence in log-probabilities.

Finally, we establish convergence in the span seminorm of
log-probabilities. Define
$`C_{t}\triangleq\|\log\pi_{t}-\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}`$.
A first-order optimality analysis of the regularized best response in
$`V_{t}`$ shows that, for each context $`x`$, the best response to
$`\pi_{t+1}`$ (denoted $`\nu_{t+1}`$) satisfies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\beta(1+1/\eta)\log\nu_{t+1}(y|x)=\beta\log\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(y|x)+(\beta/\eta)\log\pi_{t}(y|x)-\mathcal{P}(\pi_{t+1}(x)\succ y\mid x)+c_{t+1}(x)\,,
``` |  |

where $`c_{t+1}(x)`$ is a normalizing constant. Similarly, the
regularized VNW $`\pi^{\star}_{\beta}`$ satisfies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\beta(1+1/\eta)\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y|x)=\beta\log\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(y|x)+(\beta/\eta)\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y|x)-\mathcal{P}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(x)\succ y\mid x)+c^{\star}(x)\,.
``` |  |

Subtracting these two expressions and computing
$`\mathrm{sp},\rho`$-norm yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\beta(1+1/\eta)\|\log\nu_{t+1}-\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}\leq(\beta/\eta)\|\log\pi_{t}-\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}+\|\mathcal{P}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}-\pi_{t+1}\succ\cdot)\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}.
``` |  |

Using
Lemma [2](#Thmlemma2 "Lemma 2 (Lipschitz property of preferences). ‣ B.1 Preference Properties ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
and Pinsker’s inequality, one can bound

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\mathcal{P}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}-\pi_{t+1}\succ\cdot)\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|\pi_{t+1})=\frac{1}{2}A_{t+1}^{2}.
``` |  |

Moreover, comparing the gradient expression

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\zeta_{t+1}(x,y)=\nabla_{\pi}V_{t}(\pi_{t+1},\pi_{t+1})(x,y)=\mathcal{P}(\pi_{t+1}(x)\succ y\mid x)+\beta\log\frac{\pi_{t+1}(y|x)}{\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(y|x)}+\beta/\eta\log\frac{\pi_{t+1}(y|x)}{\pi_{t}(y|x)}+d_{t+1}(x){\bf 1}\,,
``` |  |

with the best-response equation for $`\nu_{t+1}`$ shows that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\zeta_{t+1}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}=\beta(1+1/\eta)\|\log\nu_{t+1}-\log\pi_{t+1}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}\,.
``` |  |

Thus, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\beta(1+1/\eta)\lVert\log\pi_{t+1}-\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}\leq(\beta/\eta)\lVert\log\pi_{t}-\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}+A_{t+1}\cdot\sqrt{1/2}+\lVert\zeta_{t+1}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}\,.
``` |  |

or, after rearranging and dividing by $`\beta(1+1/\eta)`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
C_{t+1}\leq\frac{1}{1+\eta}C_{t}+\frac{A_{t+1}}{\beta(1+1/\eta)\cdot\sqrt{2}}+\frac{\lVert\zeta_{t+1}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}}{\beta(1+1/\eta)}\,.
``` |  |

Taking the square and applying the inequality
$`(a+b)^{2}\leq(1+\alpha)a^{2}+(1+1/\alpha)b^{2}`$ twice: first time
with $`\alpha=\eta`$ and the second one with $`\alpha=1`$ implies

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle C_{t+1}^{2}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1+\eta}C_{t}^{2}+\frac{1}{\beta^{2}\cdot(1+1/\eta)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{A_{t+1}}{\sqrt{2}}+\lVert\zeta_{t+1}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}}}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{1+\eta}C_{t}^{2}+\frac{\eta\cdot A_{t+1}^{2}}{\beta^{2}(1+\eta)}+\frac{2\eta\cdot\lVert\zeta_{t+1}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}}{\beta^{2}(1+\eta)}\,.`$ |  |

Plugging in the bound on $`A_{t+1}^{2}`$ and
$`\lVert\zeta_{t+1}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}`$ implies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
C_{t+1}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{1+\eta}C_{t}^{2}+\frac{\eta\cdot\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|\pi_{0})}{\beta^{2}(1+\eta)}(1+\eta/2)^{-(t+1)}+\frac{2\eta(1+\beta^{2})\cdot\varepsilon}{\beta^{4}(1+\eta)}
``` |  |

Unrolling this recursion, we achieve for any $`t\in\mathbb{N}`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle C_{t}^{2}\leq(1+\eta)^{-t}C_{0}^{2}+\frac{2\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|\pi_{0})}{\beta^{2}}\cdot(1+\eta/2)^{-t}+\frac{2\eta(1+\beta^{2})\cdot\varepsilon}{\beta^{4}(1+\eta)}\cdot\frac{1}{1-1/(1+\eta)}\,,`$ |  |

and simplifying the last term we conclude the statement. ∎

## Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients

In this section, we study a symmetric preference game with KL
regularization that appears as a single inner problem of the proximal
point method:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\min_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathcal{P}^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi\succ\pi^{\prime})\triangleq\mathcal{P}(\pi\succ\pi^{\prime})-\lambda\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\widetilde{\pi})+\lambda\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\prime}\|\widetilde{\pi})}}\right\}\,.
``` |  | (24) |

We solve this sub-problem using a self-play policy gradient method.

##### Policy parametrization.

We consider a general policy parameterization: for a parameter
$`\theta\in\Theta\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}`$ let
$`\theta\mapsto\pi_{\theta}\in\Pi`$ be a corresponding policy. A
canonical example is the standard softmax parametrization with
$`\Theta=\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}}`$ and
$`\pi_{\theta}(y|x)\propto{\rm e}^{\theta_{x,y}}`$.

##### Best-response objective.

For an arbitrary competitor policy $`\pi\in\Pi`$ and a parameter
$`\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}`$, define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi)\triangleq\mathcal{P}(\pi\succ\pi_{\theta})+\lambda\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{\theta}\|\widetilde{\pi})\,.
``` |  |

We will repeatedly relate $`J^{\widetilde{\pi}}`$ to the value function
$`V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}`$ and its best-response value
$`V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}`$ (see
Appendix [B](#A2 "Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")).

The corresponding parametrized best-response operator and value are
defined as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\theta^{\star}_{\pi}\triangleq\argmin_{\theta\in\Theta}J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi),\qquad J^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}(\pi)\triangleq\min_{\theta\in\Theta}J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi)\,.
``` |  | (25) |

Our objective instead is to minimize the exploitability gap (or
suboptimality)
$`\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta})=\max_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi}\{\tfrac{1}{2}-\mathcal{P}^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta}\succ\pi^{\prime})\}`$.
Mirror Prox approximates the solution to
([24](#A3.E24 "In Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
by two applications of the best-response operator. However, it requires
solving
([25](#A3.E25 "In Best-response objective. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
exactly, which is infeasible beyond the tabular setting.

##### Best-response approximation via gradient step.

Rather than solving
([25](#A3.E25 "In Best-response objective. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
exactly, we approximate it using a single gradient step:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\theta_{t+1}=\mathcal{T}(\theta_{t}-\gamma_{t}\cdot g_{t})\,,\qquad\pi_{t+1}=\pi_{\theta_{t+1}}\,,
``` |  | (26) |

where $`(\gamma_{t})_{t\geq 0}`$ is a sequence of step-sizes,
$`\mathcal{T}`$ is a projection-like operator that guarantees policy
improvement, and $`g_{t}`$ is a stochastic estimator of
$`\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t})`$ computed from a
mini-batch of size $`B_{t}\geq 1`$. For an example of $`g_{t}`$, we
refer to
Appendix [C.2](#A3.SS2 "C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").

##### Parametrization assumptions.

Let
$`\Theta_{\mathcal{T}}\triangleq\{\mathcal{T}(\theta)\mid\theta\in\Theta\}`$
denote the image of the improvement operator $`\mathcal{T}`$, and assume
$`\theta_{0}\in\Theta_{\mathcal{T}}`$. Following ([Yuan et al.,
2022](#bib.bib57)), we provide a set of assumptions on the
parametrization $`\theta\mapsto\pi_{\theta}`$.

###### Assumption 2 (Lipschitzness of parametrization).

For all $`\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\Theta`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lVert\pi_{\theta}-\pi_{\theta^{\prime}}\rVert_{1,\rho}\leq G\lVert\theta-\theta^{\prime}\rVert_{2}
``` |  |

###### Assumption 3 (Smoothness).

For any $`\pi\in\Pi`$, the function $`J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi)`$
is $`L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$-smooth, i.e., for all
$`\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\Theta`$:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
-\frac{L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{2}\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|^{2}_{2}\leq J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta^{\prime};\pi)-J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi)-\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi),\theta^{\prime}-\theta\rangle\leq\frac{L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{2}\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|^{2}_{2}\,,
``` |  |

We notice that smoothness implies the following useful inequality (see,
e.g., [Nesterov and others 2018, Theorem 2.1.5](#bib.bib61))

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})\|^{2}_{2}\leq 2L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}(J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})-J^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}(\pi_{\theta}))\leq 2L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\cdot\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta})\,.
``` |  |

###### Assumption 4 (Approximate Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality).

The function $`J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi)`$ satisfies the
approximate version of Polyak–Łojasiewicz (PL) inequality: there exists
a constant
$`m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\in(0,L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}]`$ such
that for any $`\theta\in\Theta_{\mathcal{T}}`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}+\|\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})\|^{2}_{2}\geq 2m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\cdot\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta})\,.
``` |  |

In particular, additive error $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}`$ naturally
appears in this bound since, in general,
$`V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta})\not=J^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}(\pi_{\theta})`$
and, thus, we can guarantee only

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta})=V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta};\pi_{\theta})-V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta})\geq J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})-J^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}(\pi_{\theta})\,,
``` |  |

since the result of minimization of value over
$`\Pi_{\Theta}\triangleq\{\pi_{\theta}\}_{\theta\in\Theta}`$ and the
full policy class $`\Pi`$ might be different.

###### Assumption 5 (Improvement operator).

For any $`\theta\in\Theta`$, the operator $`\mathcal{T}`$ does not
increase exploitability:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\mathcal{T}(\theta)})\leq\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta})\,.
``` |  |

This assumption is always satisfied by
$`\mathcal{T}=\operatorname{Id}`$; however, for some particular cases,
it is necessary to consider non-trivial $`\mathcal{T}`$ to satisfy other
assumptions (primarily
Assumption [4](#Thmassumption4 "Assumption 4 (Approximate Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")).
We notice that $`\mathcal{T}`$ is *not* a projection in the usual sense:
since our objective
$`\theta\mapsto\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta})`$ is
non-convex, it means that a usual (Euclidean) projection may be
detrimental for the current optimization progress.

###### Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise).

Let $`(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\geq 0}`$ be the filtration induced by the
algorithm iterates:
$`\mathcal{F}_{t}\triangleq\sigma(\{\theta_{j},\pi_{j}\}_{j\leq t})`$.
For each $`t`$, let $`B_{t}\geq 1`$ denote the mini-batch size used to
construct $`g_{t}`$, and define the gradient bias and noise

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\xi_{t}\triangleq g_{t}-\mathbb{E}[g_{t}|\mathcal{F}_{t}]\,,\qquad b_{t}\triangleq\mathbb{E}[g_{t}|\mathcal{F}_{t}]-\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t})\,.
``` |  |

Then, for any $`t\in\mathbb{N}`$, conditionally on $`\mathcal{F}_{t}`$,
the following holds:

- (i)
  Bounded bias:

  |     |                                                          |     |
  |-----|----------------------------------------------------------|-----|
  |     |
         ``` math
         \lVert b_{t}\rVert_{2}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}\,.
         ```                                                       |     |
- (ii)
  Subgaussian tails with variance proxy scaling as $`1/B_{t}`$: there
  exists a constant $`\sigma^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}>0`$ such that
  for all $`u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}`$ and $`s\in\mathbb{R}`$,

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \log\mathbb{E}\big[\exp\big(s\cdot\langle u,\xi_{t}\rangle\big)\big|\mathcal{F}_{t}\big]\leq\frac{s^{2}\sigma^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\cdot\lVert u\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2B_{t}}\,.
  ``` |  |
- (iii)
  Subexponential tails for the squared norm with variance proxy scaling
  as $`1/B_{t}`$: there exists a constant
  $`v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}>0`$ such that for all
  $`s\in[0,B_{t}/v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}]`$,

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \log\mathbb{E}\big[\exp\big(s\lVert\xi_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\big)\big|\,\mathcal{F}_{t}\big]\leq s\cdot\frac{v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{B_{t}}\,.
  ``` |  |

###### Remark 2.

Assumption [6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
captures the standard $`1/B_{t}`$ variance reduction from averaging a
mini-batch of $`B_{t}`$ independent samples. For example, suppose
$`g_{t}=\frac{1}{B_{t}}\sum_{i=1}^{B_{t}}\hat{g}(\theta_{t},\pi_{\theta_{t}};Z_{t,i})`$,
where conditionally on $`\mathcal{F}_{t}`$ the random variables
$`\{Z_{t,i}\}_{i=1}^{B_{t}}`$ are independent and the single-sample
noises
$`\hat{g}(\theta_{t},\pi_{\theta_{t}};Z_{t,i})-\mathbb{E}_{Z_{t,i}}[\hat{g}(\theta_{t},\pi_{\theta_{t}};Z_{t,i})]`$
are subgaussian with variance proxy $`\bar{\sigma}^{2}`$ (possibly
depending on the dimension). Then the averaged noise $`\xi_{t}`$ is
subgaussian with variance proxy $`\bar{\sigma}^{2}/B_{t}`$, and
$`\lVert\xi_{t}\rVert^{2}`$ is also subexponential of the form
$`\bar{v}^{2}/B_{t}`$ for some $`\bar{v}^{2}>0`$ (see, e.g., [Jin et al.
2019](#bib.bib58)). This covers, in particular, the cases of bounded
per-sample gradients and purely Gaussian noise.

In
Appendix [C.3](#A3.SS3 "C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
we explicitly verify these assumptions of a simple softmax
parametrization, and in
Appendix [C.4](#A3.SS4 "C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
we verify them for a general family of policies under compatible
Fisher-nondegenerate parameterization.

### C.1 Convergence Guarantees

In the following, we analyze two cases separately: the deterministic and
the stochastic. Before that, we prove the following important result,
which we split into two parts for the sake of better result exposition.

###### Lemma 7 (Descent Lemma I).

Assume
Assumptions [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Lipschitzness of parametrization). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-[3](#Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3 (Smoothness). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-[5](#Thmassumption5 "Assumption 5 (Improvement operator). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
For the iterates of policy-gradient
self-play ([26](#A3.E26 "In Best-response approximation via gradient step. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")),
for any sequence of learning rates $`(\gamma_{t})_{t\geq 0}`$ and for
any $`t\geq 0`$, it holds that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\sqrt{\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t+1})}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}G^{2}}{8\lambda}\lVert g_{t}\rVert^{2}_{2}}+\sqrt{\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})-\gamma_{t}\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),g_{t}\big\rangle+\Bigl(L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}+\frac{G^{2}}{4\lambda}\Bigr)\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}}{2}\|g_{t}\|^{2}_{2}}\,.
``` |  |

###### Remark 3 (On the additional term.).

Compared to standard descent-lemma arguments for a fixed smooth
objective,
Lemma [7](#Thmlemma7 "Lemma 7 (Descent Lemma I). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
contains an extra term. This term reflects that the best-response
objective is *dynamic*: after each update, the competitor policy
$`\pi_{t}`$ changes, so the objective function
$`\theta\mapsto J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{t})`$ shifts across
iterations.

###### Proof.

We decompose one iteration
of ([26](#A3.E26 "In Best-response approximation via gradient step. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
as

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\theta_{t}^{+}\triangleq\theta_{t}-\gamma_{t}g_{t},\qquad\theta_{t+1}=\mathcal{T}(\theta_{t}^{+}),\qquad\pi_{t}^{+}\triangleq\pi_{\theta_{t}^{+}}\,.
``` |  |

Step 1: Smoothness in $`\theta`$. By
Assumption [3](#Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3 (Smoothness). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
for any $`t\in\mathbb{N}`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t}^{+};\pi_{t})\leq J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t})+\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),\theta_{t}^{+}-\theta_{t}\big\rangle+\frac{L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{2}\|\theta_{t}^{+}-\theta_{t}\|^{2}_{2}\,.
``` |  |

Using $`\theta_{t}^{+}-\theta_{t}=-\gamma_{t}g_{t}`$, we obtain

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t}^{+};\pi_{t})\leq J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t})-\gamma_{t}\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),g_{t}\big\rangle+\frac{L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\gamma^{2}_{t}}{2}\|g_{t}\|^{2}_{2}\,.
``` |  | (27) |

Subtracting $`V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})`$ from both
sides and using
Lemma [3](#Thmlemma3 "Lemma 3 (Exploitability gap via value function). ‣ B.2 Regularized Preference and Value ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
which gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t})-V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})=V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t};\pi_{t})-V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})=\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})\,,
``` |  |

we obtain

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+};\pi_{t})-V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})\leq\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})-\gamma_{t}\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),g_{t}\big\rangle+\frac{L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\gamma^{2}_{t}}{2}\|g_{t}\|^{2}_{2}\,.
``` |  | (28) |

Step 2: Relating $`J^{\widetilde{\pi}}`$ to
$`\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+})`$. Using the identity
$`\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta})=V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta};\pi_{\theta})-V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta})`$,
we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+};\pi_{t})-V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})=\underbrace{V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+};\pi_{t}^{+})-V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+})}_{\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+})}+\bigl(V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+};\pi_{t})-V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+};\pi_{t}^{+})\bigr)+\bigl(V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+})-V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})\bigr)\,.
``` |  |

Substituting this identity
into ([28](#A3.E28 "In Proof. ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
yields

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\begin{split}\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+})\leq\;&\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})-\gamma_{t}\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),g_{t}\big\rangle+\frac{L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\gamma_{t}^{2}}{2}\|g_{t}\|^{2}_{2}\\
&\quad+\bigl(V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+};\pi_{t}^{+})-V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+};\pi_{t})\bigr)+\bigl(V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})-V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+})\bigr)\,.\end{split}
``` |  | (29) |

By definition of $`V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+};\pi_{t}^{+})-V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+};\pi_{t})=\mathcal{P}(\pi_{t}^{+}-\pi_{t}\succ\pi_{t}^{+})\,.
``` |  |

Moreover, the lower bound of
Lemma [5](#Thmlemma5 "Lemma 5 (Smoothness of the best-response value). ‣ B.2 Regularized Preference and Value ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+})-V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})\geq\mathcal{P}(\pi_{t}^{+}-\pi_{t}\succ\nu_{t}^{+})\,,
``` |  |

where $`\nu_{t}^{+}`$ is a best response to $`\pi_{t}^{+}`$. Thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})-V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+})\leq-\mathcal{P}(\pi_{t}^{+}-\pi_{t}\succ\nu_{t}^{+})\,.
``` |  |

Substituting these
into ([29](#A3.E29 "In Proof. ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
and using bilinearity of $`\mathcal{P}`$, we obtain

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+})\leq\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})-\gamma_{t}\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),g_{t}\big\rangle+\frac{L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\gamma_{t}^{2}}{2}\|g_{t}\|^{2}_{2}+\mathcal{P}(\pi_{t}^{+}-\pi_{t}\succ\pi_{t}^{+}-\nu_{t}^{+})\,.
``` |  | (30) |

Step 3: Bounding the preference term.
Lemma [2](#Thmlemma2 "Lemma 2 (Lipschitz property of preferences). ‣ B.1 Preference Properties ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
implies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{P}(\pi_{t}^{+}-\pi_{t}\succ\pi_{t}^{+}-\nu_{t}^{+})\leq\frac{1}{2}\lVert\pi_{t}^{+}-\pi_{t}\rVert_{1,\rho}\cdot\lVert\nu_{t}^{+}-\pi_{t}^{+}\rVert_{1,\rho}\,.
``` |  |

Assumption [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Lipschitzness of parametrization). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
gives
$`\lVert\pi_{t}^{+}-\pi_{t}\rVert_{1,\rho}\leq G\lVert\theta_{t}^{+}-\theta_{t}\rVert_{2}=G\gamma_{t}\lVert g_{t}\rVert_{2}`$.
By Pinsker’s inequality and
Lemma [4](#Thmlemma4 "Lemma 4 (Strong convexity in 𝜋 with respect to KL_𝜌). ‣ B.2 Regularized Preference and Value ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lVert\nu_{t}^{+}-\pi_{t}^{+}\rVert_{1,\rho}^{2}\leq 2\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{t}^{+}\|\nu_{t}^{+})\leq\frac{2}{\lambda}\cdot\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+})\,,\quad\text{ so }\quad\lVert\nu_{t}^{+}-\pi_{t}^{+}\rVert_{1,\rho}\leq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\lambda}\,\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+})}\,.
``` |  |

Consequently,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{P}(\pi_{t}^{+}-\pi_{t}\succ\pi_{t}^{+}-\nu_{t}^{+})\leq\frac{G\gamma_{t}}{2}\lVert g_{t}\rVert_{2}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{2}{\lambda}\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+})}\,.
``` |  |

As a
result, ([30](#A3.E30 "In Proof. ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
becomes

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\begin{split}\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+})\leq\;&\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})-\gamma_{t}\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),g_{t}\big\rangle+\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}\cdot L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{2}\|g_{t}\|^{2}_{2}\\
&\quad+\frac{G\gamma_{t}}{2}\lVert g_{t}\rVert_{2}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{2}{\lambda}\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+})}\,.\end{split}
``` |  | (31) |

Let
$`S\triangleq\sqrt{\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+})}`$.
Then
([31](#A3.E31 "In Proof. ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
can be written as

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
S^{2}-2S\sqrt{\frac{G^{2}\gamma_{t}^{2}}{8\lambda}\lVert g_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}}\leq\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})-\gamma_{t}\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),g_{t}\big\rangle+\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}\cdot L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{2}\|g_{t}\|^{2}_{2}\,.
``` |  |

Adding
$`\frac{G^{2}\gamma_{t}^{2}}{8\lambda}\lVert g_{t}\rVert^{2}_{2}`$ to
both sides and completing the square yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\biggl(S-\sqrt{\frac{G^{2}\gamma_{t}^{2}}{8\lambda}\lVert g_{t}\rVert^{2}_{2}}\biggr)^{2}\leq\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})-\gamma_{t}\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),g_{t}\big\rangle+\Bigl(L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}+\frac{G^{2}}{4\lambda}\Bigr)\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}}{2}\|g_{t}\|^{2}_{2}\,.
``` |  |

Taking square roots, we get

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sqrt{\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+})}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}G^{2}}{8\lambda}\lVert g_{t}\rVert^{2}_{2}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})-\gamma_{t}\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),g_{t}\big\rangle+\Bigl(L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}+\frac{G^{2}}{4\lambda}\Bigr)\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}}{2}\|g_{t}\|^{2}_{2}}\,.`$ |  |

By
Assumption [5](#Thmassumption5 "Assumption 5 (Improvement operator). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
$`\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t+1})\leq\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+})`$,
so

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sqrt{\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t+1})}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}G^{2}}{8\lambda}\lVert g_{t}\rVert^{2}_{2}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})-\gamma_{t}\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),g_{t}\big\rangle+\Bigl(L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}+\frac{G^{2}}{4\lambda}\Bigr)\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}}{2}\|g_{t}\|^{2}_{2}}\,.`$ |  |

∎

###### Lemma 8 (Descent Lemma II).

Assume
Assumptions [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Lipschitzness of parametrization). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-[3](#Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3 (Smoothness). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-[4](#Thmassumption4 "Assumption 4 (Approximate Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-[5](#Thmassumption5 "Assumption 5 (Improvement operator). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
and $`\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\geq G^{2}`$. For the iterates
of policy-gradient
self-play ([26](#A3.E26 "In Best-response approximation via gradient step. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")),
for any sequence of learning rates $`(\gamma_{t})_{t\geq 0}`$ satisfying
$`\gamma_{t}\leq 1/(2L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}),`$ and for any
$`t\geq 0`$, it holds that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t+1})`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(1-\frac{\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{2}\Bigr)\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})-\alpha_{t}\,\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),\xi_{t}\big\rangle`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{3L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{2}\cdot\gamma_{t}^{2}(\|\xi_{t}\|^{2}_{2}+\|b_{t}\|_{2}^{2})+\frac{3G^{2}}{4\lambda\,m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\cdot\gamma_{t}(\|\xi_{t}\|^{2}_{2}+\|b_{t}\|_{2}^{2})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{3}{4}\cdot\gamma_{t}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}+\lVert b_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2})\,,`$ |  |

where $`\xi_{t}\triangleq g_{t}-\mathbb{E}[g_{t}|\mathcal{F}_{t}]`$ and
$`b_{t}\triangleq\mathbb{E}[g_{t}|\mathcal{F}_{t}]-\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t})`$
are the gradient noise and bias respectively,
$`m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$ is defined in
Assumption [4](#Thmassumption4 "Assumption 4 (Approximate Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\alpha_{t}\triangleq\frac{1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}/2}{1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\cdot\gamma_{t}\cdot\Bigl(1-\gamma_{t}L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-\frac{G^{2}}{2\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\Bigr)\,.
``` |  |

###### Remark 4.

In this Lemma, we have introduced an additional condition on $`\lambda`$
to be a large enough constant. Although hypothetically it might be
possible that $`m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$ decreasing with a value of
$`\lambda`$, in any known examples $`m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$
non-decreases with an increase of $`\lambda`$, making an inequality
$`\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\geq 0`$ solvable. We refer to
Proposition [5](#Thmproposition5 "Proposition 5 (Softmax parametrization satisfies Assumptions –). ‣ Parametrization. ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
and
Proposition [6](#Thmproposition6 "Proposition 6 (Compatible Fisher non-degenerate parameterization satisfies Assumptions –). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
for examples.

###### Proof.

Starting from
Lemma [7](#Thmlemma7 "Lemma 7 (Descent Lemma I). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
one can take squares and apply an inequality
$`(a+b)^{2}\leq(1+1/A_{t})a^{2}+(1+A_{t})b^{2}`$ for any $`A_{t}>0`$:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t+1})\leq\frac{1+A_{t}}{A_{t}}\cdot\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}G^{2}}{8\lambda}\lVert g_{t}\rVert^{2}_{2}`$ |  | (32) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+(1+A_{t})\biggl(\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})-\gamma_{t}\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),g_{t}\big\rangle+\Bigl(L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}+\frac{G^{2}}{4\lambda}\Bigr)\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}}{2}\|g_{t}\|^{2}_{2}\biggr)`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=(1+A_{t})\biggl(\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})-\gamma_{t}\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),g_{t}\big\rangle+\Bigl(L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}+\frac{G^{2}}{4\lambda}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{A_{t}}}}\right)\Bigr)\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}}{2}\|g_{t}\|^{2}_{2}\biggr)\,.`$ |  |  |

To simplify expressions, we also upper bound $`1+1/A_{t}`$ by
$`2+1/A_{t}`$ in the last term. The reasons for this loose bound will be
more evident lately.

Define the gradient noise
$`\xi_{t}\triangleq g_{t}-\mathbb{E}[g_{t}|\mathcal{F}_{t}]`$ and bias
$`b_{t}\triangleq\mathbb{E}[g_{t}|\mathcal{F}_{t}]-\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t})`$.
Then

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lVert g_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}=\lVert\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t})+b_{t}+\xi_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq\lVert\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t})\rVert^{2}_{2}+2\lVert\xi_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}+2\lVert b_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}+2\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),\xi_{t}+b_{t}\big\rangle\,.
``` |  |

Substituting this
into ([32](#A3.E32 "In Proof. ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
yields

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t+1})`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq(1+A_{t})\Bigl(\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})-\gamma_{t}\bigl(1-\tfrac{\gamma_{t}}{2}(L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}+\tfrac{G^{2}}{4\lambda}\cdot\tfrac{2A_{t}+1}{A_{t}})\bigr)\big\|\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t})\big\|_{2}^{2}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt-\gamma_{t}\bigl(1-\gamma_{t}(L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}+\tfrac{G^{2}}{4\lambda}\cdot\tfrac{2A_{t}+1}{A_{t}}\bigr)\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),b_{t}\big\rangle`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt-\gamma_{t}\bigl(1-\gamma_{t}(L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}+\tfrac{G^{2}}{4\lambda}\cdot\tfrac{2A_{t}+1}{A_{t}}\bigr)\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),\xi_{t}\big\rangle`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt+\Bigl(L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}+\tfrac{G^{2}}{4\lambda}\cdot\tfrac{2A_{t}+1}{A_{t}}\Bigr)\gamma_{t}^{2}(\|\xi_{t}\|^{2}_{2}+\lVert b_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2})\Bigr)\,.`$ |  |

Now, we set

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
A_{t}=\frac{\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{2(1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})}\,,
``` |  |

where $`m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$ is defined in
Assumption [4](#Thmassumption4 "Assumption 4 (Approximate Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
Note that $`A_{t}`$ is positive since
$`\gamma_{t}\leq 1/(2L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})\leq 1/(2m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})`$
thanks to
Assumption [3](#Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3 (Smoothness). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
Assumption [4](#Thmassumption4 "Assumption 4 (Approximate Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
One checks that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
1+A_{t}=\frac{1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}/2}{1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}},\qquad\frac{1+2A_{t}}{4\lambda A_{t}}=\frac{1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{2\lambda\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}+\frac{1}{2\lambda}=\frac{1}{2\lambda\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\,.
``` |  |

Simplifying the constants, we obtain

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\begin{split}\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t+1})&\leq\frac{1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}/2}{1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\Bigl(\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\gamma_{t}\Bigl(1-\frac{\gamma_{t}L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{2}-\frac{G^{2}}{4\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\Bigr)\big\|\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t})\big\|_{2}^{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\gamma_{t}\Bigl(1-\gamma_{t}L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-\frac{G^{2}}{2\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\Bigr)\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),b_{t}\big\rangle\Bigr)\end{split}`$ |  | $`\displaystyle\triangleq\mathbf{(A)}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}/2}{1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\gamma_{t}\Bigl(1-\gamma_{t}L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-\frac{G^{2}}{2\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\Bigr)\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),\xi_{t}\big\rangle`$ | $`\displaystyle\triangleq\mathbf{(B)}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}/2}{1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\Bigl(L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}+\frac{G^{2}}{2\lambda\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\Bigr)\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}}{2}\|\xi_{t}+b_{t}\|^{2}_{2}\,.`$ | $`\displaystyle\triangleq\mathbf{(C)}`$ |  |

Next, we verify that under our assumptions, the first (deterministic)
term contracts. First, we verify that
$`1-\gamma_{t}\cdot L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-G^{2}/(2\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})\geq 0`$.
Indeed, since $`\gamma_{t}\leq 1/(2L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})`$ and
$`\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\geq G^{2}`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
1-\gamma_{t}\cdot L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-\frac{G^{2}}{2\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\geq 1-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\geq 0\,.
``` |  |

Thus, the coefficient in front of
$`\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),b_{t}\big\rangle`$
is non-negative. By Cauchy–Schwarz and an inequality
$`ab\leq a^{2}/2+b^{2}/2`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle-\gamma_{t}\Bigl(1-\gamma_{t}\cdot L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-\tfrac{G^{2}}{2\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\Bigr)\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),b_{t}\big\rangle`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{t}\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{\gamma_{t}\cdot L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{2}-\tfrac{G^{2}}{4\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\Bigr)\big\|\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t})\big\|_{2}^{2}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{\gamma_{t}}{2}\|b_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\,,`$ |  |

and substituting this into $`\mathbf{(A)}`$ yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbf{(A)}\leq\frac{1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}/2}{1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\Bigl(\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})-\frac{\gamma_{t}}{2}\big\|\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t})\big\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\gamma_{t}}{2}\|b_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr)\,.
``` |  |

Thus, the coefficient in front of
$`\big\|\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t})\big\|_{2}^{2}`$
is positive. By
Assumption [4](#Thmassumption4 "Assumption 4 (Approximate Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\big\|\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t})\big\|_{2}^{2}\geq 2m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\,\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})-\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}\,,
``` |  |

therefore $`\mathbf{(A)}`$ can be bounded as

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbf{(A)}\leq(1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}/2)\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})+\frac{1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}/2}{1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\gamma_{t}}{2}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}+\frac{\gamma_{t}}{2}\|b_{t}\|_{2}^{2}}}\right)\,.
``` |  |

To bound the last term, we use the fact that since
$`L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\geq m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$, we have
$`\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\leq 1/2`$ and thus
$`(1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}/2)\leq 3/2\cdot(1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})`$.

For $`\mathbf{(B)}`$, since the sign of
$`\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),\xi_{t}\rangle`$
can be arbitrary, we cannot provide a clear upper bound on this term and
we only define a coefficient in front of it as $`\alpha_{t}`$:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbf{(B)}=-\alpha_{t}\cdot\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),\xi_{t}\rangle
``` |  |

For $`\mathbf{(C)}`$, we also use the bound
$`(1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}/2)\leq 3/2\cdot(1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})`$,
thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbf{(C)}\leq\frac{3L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{2}\cdot\gamma_{t}^{2}(\lVert\xi_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}+\lVert b_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2})+\frac{3G^{2}}{4\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\cdot\gamma_{t}(\lVert\xi_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}+\lVert b_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2})\,.
``` |  |

Combining all the bounds, we conclude the statement. ∎

###### Remark 5 (On the necessity of the $`\gamma_{t}\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}^{2}`$ term).

Lemma [8](#Thmlemma8 "Lemma 8 (Descent Lemma II). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
contains a noise contribution of order
$`\gamma_{t}\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}^{2}`$, whereas in the usual PL analysis of
SGD on a *fixed* smooth objective one typically pays only
$`\mathcal{O}(\gamma_{t}^{2}\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}^{2})`$. This
linear-in-$`\gamma_{t}`$ variance penalty is intrinsic to our setting.

The key difference is that our progress measure

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})\;=\;V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t};\pi_{t})-V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})
``` |  |

uses the best-response value
$`V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})`$ as a *moving baseline*.
After each update, the opponent changes from $`\pi_{t}`$ to
$`\pi_{t+1}`$, so $`V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})`$
drifts. Controlling this drift is exactly what produces the extra
square-root term in
Lemma [7](#Thmlemma7 "Lemma 7 (Descent Lemma I). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback");
see
([31](#A3.E31 "In Proof. ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")),
which contains (up to constants)

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\gamma_{t}\,\|g_{t}\|_{2}\,\sqrt{\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t}^{+})}.
``` |  |

With stochastic gradients
$`g_{t}=\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t})+\xi_{t}`$, this
term injects noise at the level
$`s_{t}\triangleq\sqrt{\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})}`$
as an additive perturbation $`\Theta(\gamma_{t}\|\xi_{t}\|_{2})`$.
Squaring to return to $`\subopt_{t}=s_{t}^{2}`$ produces a cross term of
size $`\Theta(\gamma_{t}\,s_{t}\|\xi_{t}\|_{2})`$. To close a recursion
in $`\subopt_{t}`$, this cross term must be absorbed into the
deterministic PL decrease, whose scale is only
$`\Theta(\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})\,s_{t}^{2}`$. Applying
Young’s inequality therefore leaves an unavoidable remainder of order
$`\Theta(\gamma_{t}/m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}^{2}`$
(with constants also involving $`1/\lambda`$ through the KL-based
control of the baseline drift), which explains the
$`\gamma_{t}\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}^{2}`$ term in
Lemma [8](#Thmlemma8 "Lemma 8 (Descent Lemma II). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").

In particular, this term vanishes when $`\xi_{t}\equiv 0`$, but in the
stochastic case it becomes the dominant noise contribution unless
$`\mathbb{E}\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}^{2}`$ decays (e.g., via growing mini-batches
as in
Assumption [6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
or via variance reduction).

###### Proposition 3 (Deterministic rates).

Assume
Assumptions [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Lipschitzness of parametrization). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-[3](#Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3 (Smoothness). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-[4](#Thmassumption4 "Assumption 4 (Approximate Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-[5](#Thmassumption5 "Assumption 5 (Improvement operator). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
and $`\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\geq G^{2}`$. For the iterates
of policy-gradient
self-play ([26](#A3.E26 "In Best-response approximation via gradient step. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
with exact gradients (i.e.,
$`g_{t}=\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t})`$), let a
sequence $`(\gamma_{t})_{t\geq 0}`$ be a constant such that
$`\gamma_{t}\equiv\gamma\leq 1/(2L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})`$ and for
any $`t\geq 0`$, it holds that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(1-\frac{\gamma m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{2}\Bigr)^{t}\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{0})+\frac{3\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}}{2m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\,,`$ |  |

where $`m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$ is defined in
Assumption [4](#Thmassumption4 "Assumption 4 (Approximate Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").

###### Proof.

Directly follows from
Lemma [8](#Thmlemma8 "Lemma 8 (Descent Lemma II). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
since in this case $`\xi_{t}\equiv b_{t}\equiv 0`$, combined with the
fact that
$`L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\geq m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$ and,
consequently, $`\gamma m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}/2<1`$ due to the
choice of a step-size. ∎

###### Proposition 4 (Stochastic rates).

Assume
Assumptions [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Lipschitzness of parametrization). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-[3](#Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3 (Smoothness). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-[4](#Thmassumption4 "Assumption 4 (Approximate Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-[5](#Thmassumption5 "Assumption 5 (Improvement operator). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
Assumption [6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
and $`\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\geq G^{2}`$. Define
$`\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=\frac{L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\geq 1`$.
Define sequences $`(\gamma_{t})_{t\geq 0}`$ and $`(B_{t})_{t\geq 0}`$ as
follows

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\gamma_{t}=\frac{4t+32\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-2}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}}=\Theta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}t}}}\right)\,,\qquad B_{t}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lceil\frac{t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}}}\right\rceil=\Theta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{t}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}}}\right)\,.
``` |  |

Let $`\delta\in(0,1)`$. Then, for the iterates of policy-gradient
self-play ([26](#A3.E26 "In Best-response approximation via gradient step. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")),
for any $`t\geq 0`$, with probability at least $`1-\delta`$ it holds
that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{64\cdot\kappa^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\log({\rm e}/\delta)}{(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-1)^{2}}\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{0})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{24\cdot\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\log({\rm e}/\delta)\cdot\log(1+t/(2\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}))}{(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-1)^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{675\cdot\sigma^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{49}+2v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}}\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{6G^{2}\cdot v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\log({\rm e}/\delta)}{\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\cdot(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-1)}+\frac{6\log({\rm e}/\delta)}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}+15/7\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2}}}\right)\,.`$ |  |

###### Remark 6 (On the growing mini-batch condition).

Compared with a standard high-probability analysis of stochastic
gradient descent under a PL condition ([Madden et al.,
2024](#bib.bib55)), our bound requires a growing mini-batch size
$`B_{t}\asymp t`$. This is not merely a technical artifact: it
compensates for a new source of noise compared to the classical PL-SGD
setting, as discussed in
Remark [5](#Thmremark5 "Remark 5 (On the necessity of the 𝛾_𝑡⁢‖𝜉_𝑡‖_2^2 term). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
In particular, the linear-in-$`\gamma_{t}`$ noise term in
Lemma [8](#Thmlemma8 "Lemma 8 (Descent Lemma II). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
would lead to a noise floor of the iterates if a constant mini-batch
size were used even with decaying learning rates.

As remarked in ([Madden et al., 2024](#bib.bib55), Appendix D), a
similar issue appears in the analysis of projected non-convex SGD, and
can be addressed either via a comparable growing mini-batch condition
([Ghadimi et al., 2016](#bib.bib59)) or via variance-reduction
techniques ([J. Reddi et al., 2016](#bib.bib60)), which we do not employ
in our setting.

###### Proof.

First, we notice that a proposed sequence of learning rates satisfies
the assumption of
Lemma [8](#Thmlemma8 "Lemma 8 (Descent Lemma II). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
Indeed,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\gamma_{t}=\frac{4t+32\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-2}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}}\leq\gamma_{0}=\frac{32\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-2}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\cdot 64\cdot\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{2m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\cdot\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}=\frac{1}{2L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\,.
``` |  |

Thus,
Lemma [8](#Thmlemma8 "Lemma 8 (Descent Lemma II). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
implies

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\begin{split}\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t+1})&\leq\Bigl(1-\frac{\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{2}\Bigr)\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})-\alpha_{t}\,\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),\xi_{t}\big\rangle\\
&\qquad+\frac{3}{2}L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\cdot\gamma_{t}^{2}(\|\xi_{t}\|^{2}_{2}+\|b_{t}\|_{2}^{2})+\frac{3G^{2}}{4\lambda\,m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\cdot\gamma_{t}(\|\xi_{t}\|^{2}_{2}+\|b_{t}\|_{2}^{2})\\
&\qquad+\frac{3}{4}\gamma_{t}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}+\lVert b_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2})\,.\end{split}
``` |  | (33) |

We notice that under our choice of $`(\gamma_{t})_{t\geq 0}`$ it holds

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
1-\frac{\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{2}=\frac{(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}-2(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})+1}{(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}}=\frac{(t-1+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}}{(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}}.
``` |  |

Thus, we can multiply both sides of
([33](#A3.E33 "In Proof. ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
by $`(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}`$ and denote
$`X_{t}=(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-1)^{2}\cdot\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})`$

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle X_{t+1}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq X_{t}-\alpha_{t}(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),\xi_{t}\big\rangle`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad+(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{3}{2}L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\gamma_{t}^{2}+\frac{3\gamma_{t}G^{2}}{4\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}}}\right)(\|\xi_{t}\|^{2}_{2}+\|b_{t}\|_{2}^{2})+\frac{3\gamma_{t}}{4}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}+\lVert b_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2})\,.`$ |  |

Next, we denote
$`Y_{t}=-\alpha_{t}(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}\big\langle\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t}),\xi_{t}\big\rangle`$
and for this bound we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle|-\alpha_{t}(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}|`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}/2}{1-\gamma_{t}m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\cdot\gamma_{t}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\gamma_{t}L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-\frac{G^{2}}{2\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}}}\right)\cdot(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{t}(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}\leq 12\cdot\frac{t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-1/2}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\leq\frac{90\cdot(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-1)}{7m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\,,`$ |  |

thus,
Assumption [6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
and
````
