````math
Assumption [4](#Thmassumption4 "Assumption 4 (Approximate Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
imply for any $`s\in\mathbb{R}`$

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\log\mathbb{E}[\exp(sY_{t})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{s^{2}}{2}\cdot(\alpha_{t}(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2})^{2}\frac{\sigma^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{B_{t}}\cdot\lVert\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta_{t};\pi_{t})\rVert_{2}^{2}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{s^{2}}{2}\cdot\underbrace{\frac{2\cdot 8100\cdot L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{49\cdot m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\cdot(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})}\cdot\sigma^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}_{\tilde{B}^{2}_{t}}\underbrace{(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-1)^{2}\subopt_{\lambda}^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\pi_{t})}_{X_{t}}\,,`$ |  |

where for the last inequality we applied
Assumption [3](#Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3 (Smoothness). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
Finally, we define

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle Z_{t}`$ | $`\displaystyle\triangleq(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{3}{2}L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\gamma_{t}^{2}+\frac{3\gamma_{t}G^{2}}{4\lambda\,m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}}}\right)\|\xi_{t}\|^{2}_{2}`$ | $`\displaystyle\triangleq Z_{t,1}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{3}{2}L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\gamma_{t}^{2}+\frac{3\gamma_{t}G^{2}}{4\lambda\,m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}}}\right)\|b_{t}\|^{2}_{2}`$ | $`\displaystyle\triangleq Z_{t,2}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{3}{4}(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}\gamma_{t}\cdot(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}+\lVert b_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2})`$ | $`\displaystyle\triangleq Z_{t,3}`$ |  |

To evaluate this tail behavior, we first bound the first term

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
Z_{t,1}=(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{3}{2}L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\gamma_{t}^{2}+\frac{3\gamma_{t}G^{2}}{4\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}}}\right)\|\xi_{t}\|^{2}_{2}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{24L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{(m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}}+\frac{3(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})G^{2}}{\lambda(m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}}}}\right)\|\xi_{t}\|^{2}_{2}\,,
``` |  |

and the similar bound of $`Z_{t,2}`$ holds, where we replace
$`\|\xi_{t}\|^{2}_{2}`$ by $`\lVert b_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}`$, however, we
perform one additional simplification that comes from a bound
$`G^{2}\leq\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$, thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
Z_{t,2}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{24\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}+\frac{3(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}}}\right)\lVert b_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,.
``` |  |

For the last term, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
Z_{t,3}=\frac{3}{4}(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}\gamma_{t}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}+\lVert b_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2})\leq\frac{3(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}+\lVert b_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2})\,.
``` |  |

thus we can apply
Assumption [6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
and achieve

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\log\,\mathbb{E}[\exp(sZ_{t})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]\leq s\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{24L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{(m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}}+\frac{3(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})G^{2}}{\lambda(m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})^{2}}}}\right)\frac{v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{B_{t}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+s\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{24\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}+\frac{3(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}}}\right)\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2}+s\cdot\frac{3(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq s\biggl[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{24\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}+\frac{3G^{2}}{\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}}}\right)v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}+\frac{24\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2}}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}+\frac{3\cdot(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}+2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2})\biggr]\triangleq s\cdot\tilde{C}_{t}`$ |  |

for a range of $`s\in[0,1/\tilde{C}_{t})`$. Thus, [Madden et al. (2024,
Theorem 9)](#bib.bib55) implies that for our system of inequalities
$`X_{t+1}\leq X_{t}+Y_{t}+Z_{t}`$, any $`t\in\mathbb{N}`$ with
probability at least $`1-\delta`$ it holds

|     |                                        |     |
|-----|----------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       X_{t}\leq K_{t}\log({\rm e}/\delta)\,,
       ```                                     |     |

where $`K_{t}`$ is a sequence that satisfies the following equations:
$`K_{t+1}^{2}\geq(K_{t}+2\tilde{C}_{t})K_{t+1}+\tilde{B}_{t}^{2}K_{t}`$
and $`K_{0}\geq X_{0}`$, which is satisfied for a sequence
$`K_{t+1}=K_{t}+2\tilde{C}_{t}+\tilde{B}_{t}^{2}`$ and
$`K_{0}=64\kappa^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{0})\geq X_{0}`$.
In particular, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle K_{t}`$ | $`\displaystyle=64\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}^{2}\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{0})+\frac{16200\cdot\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{49}\cdot\sigma^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\cdot\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{s+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}+48\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\cdot v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\cdot\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{s+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad+6t\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\cdot G^{2}}{\lambda\cdot m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}+\frac{4\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2}}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}}}\right)+\frac{6}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}+2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2}}}\right)\cdot\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}(s+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq 64\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}^{2}\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{0})+24\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\log(1+t/(2\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}))\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{675\sigma^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{49}+2v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}}\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+6t\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\cdot G^{2}}{\lambda\cdot m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}+\frac{4\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2}}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}}}\right)+\frac{6}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}+2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2}}}\right)\cdot\frac{t(t+16\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-1)}{2}\,.`$ |  |

Before proceeding further, we notice that since
$`\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\geq 1`$, we can use the following
bounds for any $`t\geq 0`$:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{t}{(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-1)^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-1}\,,\qquad\frac{4t\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-1)^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{7}\,,\qquad\frac{t(t+16\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-1)}{2(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-1)^{2}}\leq 1\,.
``` |  |

Finally, we divide both bounds on $`X_{t}`$ and $`K_{t}`$ by
$`(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-1)^{2}`$ and thus we have the
following bound for exploitability that holds for any $`t\in\mathbb{N}`$
with probability at least $`1-\delta`$

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{t})`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{64\cdot\kappa^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\log({\rm e}/\delta)}{(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-1)^{2}}\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{0})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{24\cdot\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\log({\rm e}/\delta)\cdot\log(1+t/(2\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}))}{(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-1)^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{675\cdot\sigma^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{49}+2v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}}\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{6G^{2}\cdot v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\log({\rm e}/\delta)}{\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\cdot(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}-1)}+\frac{6(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}+15/7\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2})\log({\rm e}/\delta)}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\,.`$ |  |

∎

### C.2 Gradient Estimator

As a particular instance, we employ the clipped gradient estimator that
allows to control the variance of the estimator via assumptions on the
parameterization only.

Given $`\theta\in\Theta`$, a context $`x\in\mathcal{X}`$, two actions
$`y,y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}`$, and an unbiased estimate $`p\in[0,1]`$
of $`\mathcal{P}(y\succ y^{\prime}|x)`$, define the advantage estimator

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
A^{\pi_{\theta}}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)=1/2-p+\lambda\log\frac{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}-\lambda\log\frac{\pi_{\theta}(y^{\prime}|x)}{\widetilde{\pi}(y^{\prime}|x)}\,.
``` |  |

Then, for a threshold parameter $`M>0`$, we define the clipped advantage
estimator

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\tilde{A}^{\pi_{\theta}}_{M}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)=\mathrm{clip}_{-M,M}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(A^{\pi_{\theta}}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)}}\right)=\begin{cases}-M&\text{if }A^{\pi_{\theta}}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)<-M\,,\\
A^{\pi_{\theta}}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)&\text{if }A^{\pi_{\theta}}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)\in[-M,M]\,,\\
M&\text{if }A^{\pi_{\theta}}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)>M\,.\end{cases}
``` |  |

Then we define two versions of the stochastic gradient estimator:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle G(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)`$ | $`\displaystyle\triangleq\frac{1}{2}\cdot(\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x)-\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y^{\prime}|x))A^{\pi_{\theta}}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)\,\,,`$ |  | (34) |
|  | $`\displaystyle G_{M}(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)`$ | $`\displaystyle\triangleq\frac{1}{2}\cdot(\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x)-\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y^{\prime}|x))\tilde{A}^{\pi_{\theta}}_{M}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)\,.`$ |  | (35) |

Then we have the following properties of this estimator.

###### Lemma 9.

Assume $`\lVert\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x)\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq M_{g}^{2}`$
for any
$`x\in\mathrm{supp}(\rho),y\in\mathcal{Y},\theta\in\Theta_{\mathcal{T}}`$.
Then, the stochastic gradient estimator defined in
([34](#A3.E34 "In C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
satisfies the following properties:

- •
  A gradient estimator $`G(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)`$ is unbiased, i.e.,
  for any $`\theta`$ it holds
  $`\mathbb{E}[G(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)]=\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})`$.
- •
  The clipped gradient estimator $`G_{M}(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)`$ is
  biased, and its bias satisfies

  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle\lVert\mathbb{E}[G_{M}(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)]-\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})\rVert_{2}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq 2M_{g}\lambda(\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\log\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)}-\frac{M-1/2}{2\lambda}}}\right)_{+}}}\right]`$ |  |
  |  |  | $`\displaystyle+2M_{g}\lambda{\rm e}^{-(M-1/2)/(2\lambda)}\,.`$ |  |
- •
  For any $`\theta`$, it holds almost surely that

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \lVert G_{M}(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)-\mathbb{E}[G_{M}(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)]\rVert_{2}\leq 2M_{g}\cdot M\,.
  ``` |  |

###### Proof.

For the first part of the statement, we notice that by linearity of
expectation, zero expectation of the score function
$`\mathbb{E}[\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x)]=0`$, and symmetry of
preferences, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[G(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{P}(\pi\succ y\mid x)+\lambda\log\frac{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}}}\right)}}\right]\,,`$ |  |

where $`x\sim\rho,y,y^{\prime}\sim\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)`$, and
$`\pi=\pi_{\theta}`$. The last expression is a standard REINFORCE
estimator ([Williams, 1992](#bib.bib21)) for a regularized RL problem
$`J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})`$, and it is known that the
last expression is equal to
$`\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})`$.

For the second part of the statement, we use the unbiasedness of
$`G(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)`$ and the definition of the clipped
estimator to write

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\|\mathbb{E}[G_{M}`$ | $`\displaystyle(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)]-\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})\|_{2}=\|\mathbb{E}[G_{M}(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)]-\mathbb{E}[G(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)]\|_{2}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{1}{2}\cdot(\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x)-\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y^{\prime}|x))\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{A}^{\pi_{\theta}}_{M}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)-A^{\pi_{\theta}}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)}}\right)}}\right]}}\right\|_{2}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x)-\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y^{\prime}|x)}}\right\|_{2}\cdot|\tilde{A}^{\pi_{\theta}}_{M}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)-A^{\pi_{\theta}}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)|}}\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq M_{g}\cdot\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[|\tilde{A}^{\pi_{\theta}}_{M}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)-A^{\pi_{\theta}}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)|}}\right]\,.`$ |  |

Next, we notice that by definition of the clipping operator it holds

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
|\tilde{A}^{\pi_{\theta}}_{M}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)-A^{\pi_{\theta}}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)|=(|A^{\pi_{\theta}}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)|-M)_{+}\,,
``` |  |

where $`(x)_{+}=\max\{0,x\}`$. Next, define
$`\ell_{\theta}(x,y)=\log\frac{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}`$
as the log-ratio random variable. Then, we can rewrite the advantage as

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
A^{\pi_{\theta}}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)=1/2-p+\lambda(\ell_{\theta}(x,y)-\ell_{\theta}(x,y^{\prime}))\,.
``` |  |

Assuming $`M\geq 1`$, we can use the triangle inequality to write

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(|A^{\pi_{\theta}}(x,y,y^{\prime},p)|-M)_{+}}}\right]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(|1/2-p+\lambda(\ell_{\theta}(x,y)-\ell_{\theta}(x,y^{\prime}))|-M)_{+}}}\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(|1/2-p|+\lambda|\ell_{\theta}(x,y)|+\lambda|\ell_{\theta}(x,y^{\prime})|-M)_{+}}}\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(1/2+\lambda|\ell_{\theta}(x,y)|+\lambda|\ell_{\theta}(x,y^{\prime})|-M)_{+}}}\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq 2\lambda\cdot\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(|\ell_{\theta}(x,y)|-\frac{M-1/2}{2\lambda}}}\right)_{+}}}\right]\,,`$ |  |

where in the last inequality we used the fact that
$`(a+b-c)_{+}\leq(a-c/2)_{+}+(b-c/2)_{+}`$ for any $`a,b,c\geq 0`$, and
the fact that $`y`$ and $`y^{\prime}`$ are i.i.d. samples from
$`\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)`$. Next, defining
$`M^{\prime}=(M-1/2)/(2\lambda)`$, we can further bound

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(|\ell_{\theta}(x,y)|-M^{\prime}}}\right)_{+}}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\ell_{\theta}(x,y)-M^{\prime}}}\right)_{+}}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\ell_{\theta}(x,y)-M^{\prime}}}\right)_{+}}}\right]\,.
``` |  |

For the first term, we apply the following inequality;
$`\ell_{\theta}(x,y)=\log\frac{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}\leq\log\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}\leq\log\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)}`$
for any $`x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y}`$ since
$`\pi_{\theta}(y|x)\leq 1`$. Thus, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\ell_{\theta}(x,y)-M^{\prime}}}\right)_{+}}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\log\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)}-M^{\prime}}}\right)_{+}}}\right]\,.
``` |  |

Next, we study the second term. Let us fix a context $`x\in\mathcal{X}`$
and define the set
$`\mathcal{Y}_{x}^{-}=\{y\in\mathcal{Y}:\pi_{\theta}(y|x)/\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)\leq{\rm e}^{-M^{\prime}}\}`$.
Then, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\ell_{\theta}(x,y)-M^{\prime}}}\right)_{+}}}\right]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}_{x}^{-}}\pi_{\theta}(y|x)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\log\frac{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}-M^{\prime}}}\right)}}\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}_{x}^{-}}\pi_{\theta}(y|x)\log\frac{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}{{\rm e}^{M^{\prime}}\cdot\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}}}\right]\,,`$ |  |

and, applying an inequality $`\log(x)\leq x-1`$ for any $`x\geq 0`$, we
achieve

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\ell_{\theta}(x,y)-M^{\prime}}}\right)_{+}}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}_{x}^{-}}\pi_{\theta}(y|x)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}{{\rm e}^{M^{\prime}}\cdot\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}-1}}\right)}}\right]\leq{\rm e}^{-M^{\prime}}\,.
``` |  |

Finally, the last statement of the lemma follows directly from the
definition of the clipped estimator. ∎

Before proceeding further, we notice that the bias of the clipped
estimator can be made arbitrarily small by increasing the clipping
threshold $`M`$; however, its specific range depends on the behavior of
the reference policy $`\widetilde{\pi}`$. In particular, if
$`\widetilde{\pi}`$ has a small minimum probability
$`\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)`$ for some contexts $`x`$, then the bias can
be large for moderate values of $`M`$. Thus, we need to introduce
additional assumptions on the reference policy to control this bias
effectively.

###### Lemma 10.

Assume $`\lVert\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x)\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq M_{g}^{2}`$
for any
$`x\in\mathrm{supp}(\rho),y\in\mathcal{Y},\theta\in\Theta_{\mathcal{T}}`$.
Then, for any desired bias level $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}>0`$, the
clipped gradient estimator defined in
([35](#A3.E35 "In C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
satisfies the following properties.

- •
  If the reference policy satisfies
  $`\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}[\log^{2}(1/\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x))]\leq V_{\widetilde{\pi}}`$
  for some constant $`V_{\widetilde{\pi}}>0`$, then under the choice
  $`M=M_{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})\triangleq 1/2+4\lambda^{2}M_{g}(V_{\widetilde{\pi}}+1)/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}`$
  the bias is bounded as $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}`$ and it holds

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \lVert G_{M}(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)-\mathbb{E}[G_{M}(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)]\rVert_{2}\leq D_{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})\,,
  ``` |  |

  where
  $`D_{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})\triangleq M_{g}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{8\lambda^{2}M_{g}(V_{\widetilde{\pi}}+1)}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}}}}\right)`$.
- •
  If the reference policy satisfies
  $`\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}[1/\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)]\leq D_{\widetilde{\pi}}`$
  for some constant $`D_{\widetilde{\pi}}>0`$, then under the choice
  $`M=M_{\infty}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})\triangleq 1/2+2\lambda\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2M_{g}\lambda(D_{\widetilde{\pi}}+1)}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}}}}\right)`$
  the bias is bounded as $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}`$ and it holds

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \lVert G_{M}(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)-\mathbb{E}[G_{M}(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)]\rVert_{2}\leq D_{\infty}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})\,,
  ``` |  |

  where
  $`D_{\infty}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})\triangleq M_{g}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+4\lambda\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2M_{g}\lambda(D_{\widetilde{\pi}}+1)}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}}}}\right)}}\right)`$.

###### Proof.

We start from the bias bound provided in
Lemma [9](#Thmlemma9 "Lemma 9. ‣ C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"):

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\lVert\mathbb{E}[G_{M}(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)]-\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})\rVert_{2}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq 2M_{g}\lambda\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\log\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)}-\frac{M-1/2}{2\lambda}}}\right)_{+}}}\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+2M_{g}\lambda{\rm e}^{-(M-1/2)/(2\lambda)}\,.`$ |  |

Let us define $`M^{\prime}=(M-1/2)/(2\lambda)`$ and then study the first
term inside the parentheses and represent it as

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\log\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)}-M^{\prime}}}\right)_{+}}}\right]\leq\int_{M^{\prime}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\log\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)}\geq u}}\right){\rm d}u\,.
``` |  |

For the first part of the statement, we apply Markov’s inequality to
write

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\log\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)}\geq u}}\right)\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}[\log^{2}(1/\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x))]}{u^{2}}\leq\frac{V_{\widetilde{\pi}}}{u^{2}}\,,
``` |  |

and thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\log\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)}-M^{\prime}}}\right)_{+}}}\right]\leq\int_{M^{\prime}}^{\infty}\frac{V_{\widetilde{\pi}}}{u^{2}}{\rm d}u=\frac{V_{\widetilde{\pi}}}{M^{\prime}}\,.
``` |  |

To control the bias at level $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}`$, we set
$`M^{\prime}`$ such that
$`2M_{g}\lambda(V_{\widetilde{\pi}}/M^{\prime}+{\rm e}^{-M^{\prime}})\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}`$.
Since $`{\rm e}^{-M^{\prime}}\leq 1/M^{\prime}`$, it is sufficient to
set
$`M^{\prime}=2M_{g}\lambda(V_{\widetilde{\pi}}+1)/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}`$,
which leads to the choice of $`M_{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})`$ in
the statement of the lemma.

For the second part of the statement, we again start from the integral
representation and apply Markov’s inequality to write

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\log\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)}\geq u}}\right)=\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)}\geq{\rm e}^{u}}}\right)\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}[1/\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)]}{{\rm e}^{u}}\leq\frac{D_{\widetilde{\pi}}}{{\rm e}^{u}}\,,
``` |  |

and thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\log\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)}-M^{\prime}}}\right)_{+}}}\right]\leq\int_{M^{\prime}}^{\infty}\frac{D_{\widetilde{\pi}}}{{\rm e}^{u}}{\rm d}u=D_{\widetilde{\pi}}\cdot{\rm e}^{-M^{\prime}}\,.
``` |  |

As a result, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lVert\mathbb{E}[G_{M}(\theta|x,y,y^{\prime},p)]-\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})\rVert_{2}\leq 2M_{g}\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(D_{\widetilde{\pi}}\cdot{\rm e}^{-M^{\prime}}+{\rm e}^{-M^{\prime}}}}\right)=2M_{g}\lambda(D_{\widetilde{\pi}}+1)\cdot{\rm e}^{-M^{\prime}}\,.
``` |  |

To control the bias at level $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}`$, we set
$`M^{\prime}`$ such that
$`2M_{g}\lambda(D_{\widetilde{\pi}}+1)\cdot{\rm e}^{-M^{\prime}}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}`$,
which leads to the choice of $`M_{\infty}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})`$
in the statement of the lemma.

∎

Next, we provide the properties of the corresponding mini-batch
stochastic gradient estimator. At iteration $`t`$ we draw an i.i.d.
mini-batch $`(x_{j},y_{j},y^{\prime}_{j},p_{j})_{j\in[B_{t}]}`$ with
$`x_{j}\sim\rho`$,
$`y_{j},y^{\prime}_{j}\sim\pi_{\theta_{t}}(\cdot|x_{j})`$ and $`p_{j}`$
an unbiased estimator of
$`\mathcal{P}(y_{j}\succ y^{\prime}_{j}|x_{j})`$, and set

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
g_{t}=\frac{1}{B_{t}}\sum_{j=1}^{B_{t}}G_{M_{k}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})}(\theta_{t}|x_{j},y_{j},y^{\prime}_{j},p_{j})\,,
``` |  | (36) |

where $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}`$ is a desired bias level and
$`k\in\{2,\infty\}`$ indicates which choice of the clipping threshold
from
Lemma [10](#Thmlemma10 "Lemma 10. ‣ C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
is used. Let $`(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\geq 0}`$ be the filtration generated
by the iterates:
$`\mathcal{F}_{t}=\sigma(\{\theta_{k},\pi_{k}\}_{k\leq t})`$.

###### Lemma 11.

Assume that the conditions of
Lemma [10](#Thmlemma10 "Lemma 10. ‣ C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
hold true for some desired bias level $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}>0`$
and $`k\in\{2,\infty\}`$. Then, the stochastic gradient estimator
defined in
([36](#A3.E36 "In C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
satisfies for any $`\mathcal{F}_{t}`$-measurable vector
$`u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}`$

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\forall s\in\mathbb{R}:`$ | $`\displaystyle\log\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(s\langle u,\xi_{t}\rangle}}\right)|\mathcal{F}_{t}}}\right]\leq\frac{s^{2}D_{k}^{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})}{2B_{t}}\lVert u\rVert_{2}^{2}\,,`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\forall s\in[0,B_{t}/(6D^{2}_{k}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}))]:`$ | $`\displaystyle\log\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\exp(s\lVert\xi_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2})|\mathcal{F}_{t}}}\right]\leq 6s\cdot D^{2}_{k}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})/B_{t}\,.`$ |  |

In particular, it satisfies
Assumption [6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with a bias $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}`$, subgaussian constant
$`\sigma^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=D_{k}^{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})`$
and subexponential constant
$`v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=6D_{k}^{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})`$.

###### Proof.

We have to compute an exact subgaussianity constant for a random vector
$`\xi_{t}`$ bounded almost surely by
$`D_{k}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})`$, which is standard (see, e.g.,
[Vershynin (2018)](#bib.bib23)) and we write the proof for the sake of
completeness. Let us fix a vector $`u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}`$ and define
$`\xi^{1}_{t},\ldots,\xi^{B_{t}}_{t}`$ as the corresponding components
of the noise vector
$`\xi^{j}_{t}=G_{M_{k}}(\theta_{t}|x_{j},y_{j},y^{\prime}_{j},p_{j})-\mathbb{E}[G_{M_{k}}(\theta_{t}|x_{j},y_{j},y^{\prime}_{j},p_{j})]`$.
Then, we define $`X_{i}=\langle u,\xi^{i}_{t}\rangle`$ as a centered
bounded random variable with
$`|X_{i}|\leq\lVert u\rVert_{2}D_{k}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})`$, and
thus (conditional) Hoeffding’s lemma ([Hoeffding, 1963](#bib.bib71))
implies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[{\rm e}^{sX_{i}}|\mathcal{F}_{t}]\leq\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{s^{2}\cdot\lVert u\rVert_{2}^{2}D_{k}^{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})}{2}}}\right)\qquad\forall s\in\mathbb{R}\,,
``` |  |

and, using independence, we have
$`\mathbb{E}[{\rm e}^{s\langle u,\xi_{t}\rangle}|\mathcal{F}_{t}]\leq{\rm e}^{s^{2}\lVert u\rVert_{2}^{2}D_{k}^{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})/(2B_{t})}`$.
To control the norm of $`\xi_{t}`$, we apply [Pinelis (1994, Theorem
3.5)](#bib.bib56):

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{P}[\lVert\xi_{t}\rVert_{2}\geq u|\mathcal{F}_{t}]\leq 2\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\frac{u^{2}\cdot B_{t}}{2D_{k}^{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})}}}\right)\,,
``` |  |

thus, defining $`v^{2}=D_{k}^{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})/B_{t}`$,
for a $`s>0`$ the following holds

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\exp(s\lVert\xi_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}[\exp(s\lVert\xi_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2})>u|\mathcal{F}_{t}]{\rm d}u`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq 1+\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\lVert\xi_{t}\rVert_{2}>\sqrt{\frac{1}{s}\log u}|\mathcal{F}_{t}}}\right]\leq 1+2\int_{1}^{\infty}u^{-\frac{1}{2v^{2}s}}{\rm d}u\,.`$ |  |

The right-hand side is finite as $`s<1/(4v^{2})<1/(2v^{2})`$, thus we
have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[\exp(s\lVert\xi_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2})]\leq 1+2\frac{1}{\frac{1}{2v^{2}s}-1}=\frac{1+2v^{2}s}{1-2v^{2}s}\leq\exp(6v^{2}s)\,,
``` |  |

where in the last inequality we used an inequality
$`(1+x)/(1-x)\leq\exp(3x)`$ for $`x\leq[0,1/2]`$. ∎

### C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization

In this section, we verify
Assumptions [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Lipschitzness of parametrization). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")–[6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
for the standard softmax parametrization with an appropriate improvement
operator and stochastic gradient estimator. For simplicity, we assume a
context-free setting, although a generalization to contextual setting is
straightforward due to separated optimization over any
$`x\in\mathcal{X}`$.

##### Parametrization.

We fix $`\Theta=\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{Y}}`$ and define

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\pi_{\theta}=\mathrm{softmax}(\theta),\qquad\pi_{\theta}(y)=\frac{\exp(\theta_{y})}{\sum_{y^{\prime}}\exp(\theta_{y^{\prime}})}\,.
``` |  | (37) |

We also fix a reference policy $`\nu\in\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}`$ with full
support, and define an improvement operator
$`\mathcal{T}:=\mathcal{T}^{\nu}_{\tau}`$ in
Section [C.3.2](#A3.SS3.SSS2 "C.3.2 Improvement Operator and Uniform PL ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
below, where $`\tau\in(0,\tau_{0}]`$ is chosen as in
Lemma [16](#Thmlemma16 "Lemma 16. ‣ C.3.2 Improvement Operator and Uniform PL ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
We will show that this choice of $`\mathcal{T}`$ satisfies
Assumption [5](#Thmassumption5 "Assumption 5 (Improvement operator). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
and yields a uniform PL constant for
Assumption [4](#Thmassumption4 "Assumption 4 (Approximate Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").

###### Proposition 5 (Softmax parametrization satisfies Assumptions [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Lipschitzness of parametrization). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")–[6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")).

Fix any full-support reference policy $`\nu\in\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}`$, a
constant $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}>0`$, and let $`\tau=\tau_{0}`$ be
as in
Lemma [16](#Thmlemma16 "Lemma 16. ‣ C.3.2 Improvement Operator and Uniform PL ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
Consider the softmax
parametrization ([37](#A3.E37 "In Parametrization. ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
with improvement operator $`\mathcal{T}:=\mathcal{T}^{\nu}_{\tau}`$ and
stochastic gradient estimator $`g_{t}`$ defined
in ([36](#A3.E36 "In C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")).
Then,
Assumptions [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Lipschitzness of parametrization). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")–[6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
hold with constants

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle G`$ | $`\displaystyle=1,\quad L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=\tfrac{5}{2}\bigl(1+\lambda\log(1/\widetilde{\pi}_{\min})\bigr)+\lambda(4+\log|\mathcal{Y}|),\quad m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=\lambda{\rm e}^{-2/\lambda}\cdot c_{\nu}^{2},\quad\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}=0\,,`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}=\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}},\qquad\qquad\qquad\sigma^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=D_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}^{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}),\qquad\qquad\qquad v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=6D_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}^{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})\,,`$ |  |

where

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle c_{\nu}`$ | $`\displaystyle\triangleq\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-2\lVert\log\widetilde{\pi}-\log\nu\rVert_{\mathrm{sp}},\log(\nu_{\min}/(1+\nu_{\min}))}}\right\}}}\right)\cdot\nu_{\min}\,,`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle D_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}^{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})`$ | $`\displaystyle\triangleq 2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+4\lambda\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2\sqrt{2}\cdot\lambda(1+\widetilde{\pi}_{\min})}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}}}}\right)+4\lambda\log(1/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})}}\right)^{2}\,.`$ |  |

and $`\nu_{\min}=\min_{y}\nu(y)`$,
$`\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}=\min_{y}\widetilde{\pi}(y)`$.

###### Proof.

Assumption [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Lipschitzness of parametrization). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
follows from
Lemma [12](#Thmlemma12 "Lemma 12 (Lipschitz parametrization, Lemma 24 of ). ‣ C.3.1 Lipschitzness and smoothness ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
Assumption [3](#Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3 (Smoothness). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
follows from
Lemma [13](#Thmlemma13 "Lemma 13 (Smoothness, cf. Lemmas 2 and 14 of ). ‣ C.3.1 Lipschitzness and smoothness ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
Assumption [5](#Thmassumption5 "Assumption 5 (Improvement operator). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
and the uniform PL condition of
Assumption [4](#Thmassumption4 "Assumption 4 (Approximate Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
follow from
Lemmas [16](#Thmlemma16 "Lemma 16. ‣ C.3.2 Improvement Operator and Uniform PL ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
and
Corollary [3](#Thmcorollary3 "Corollary 3 (Uniform Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality). ‣ C.3.2 Improvement Operator and Uniform PL ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
Finally,
Assumption [6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
follows from
Lemma [17](#Thmlemma17 "Lemma 17. ‣ C.3.3 Gradient Estimation and Noise Bounds ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
∎

###### Corollary 2 (SPG iteration and sample complexity: tabular softmax).

Fix $`\delta\in(0,1)`$ and $`\varepsilon\in(0,1)`$. Consider the
context-free tabular softmax parametrization from
Appendix [C.3](#A3.SS3 "C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with improvement operator $`\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{T}^{\nu}_{\tau_{0}}`$
from
Lemma [16](#Thmlemma16 "Lemma 16. ‣ C.3.2 Improvement Operator and Uniform PL ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
and run
SPG ([26](#A3.E26 "In Best-response approximation via gradient step. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
with $`(\gamma_{t},B_{t})`$ as in
Proposition [4](#Thmproposition4 "Proposition 4 (Stochastic rates). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
Assume
$`\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}=\min_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\widetilde{\pi}(y)>0`$
and let
$`m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=\lambda{\rm e}^{-2/\lambda}c_{\nu}^{2}`$
be as in
Corollary [3](#Thmcorollary3 "Corollary 3 (Uniform Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality). ‣ C.3.2 Improvement Operator and Uniform PL ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
(so
$`\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=\lambda^{2}{\rm e}^{-2/\lambda}c_{\nu}^{2}`$).
Assume $`\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\geq 1`$ (e.g., it suffices
that $`\lambda\geq\max\{2,{\rm e}^{1/2}/c_{\nu}\}`$).

Choose the clipped pairwise estimator from
Lemma [17](#Thmlemma17 "Lemma 17. ‣ C.3.3 Gradient Estimation and Noise Bounds ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with bias level

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2}=\frac{7\,m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\,\varepsilon}{180\log({\rm e}/\delta)}\qquad\text{(so in particular }M=\tfrac{1}{2}+2\lambda\log\!\bigl(\tfrac{2\sqrt{2}\,\lambda(1+\widetilde{\pi}_{\min})}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}}\bigr)=\Theta(\log(1/\varepsilon))\text{)}.
``` |  |

Then with probability at least $`1-\delta`$ the iterate $`\pi_{T}`$
satisfies
$`\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{T})\leq\varepsilon`$
(equivalently, $`\pi_{T}`$ is an $`\varepsilon`$-VNW for the
$`\lambda`$-regularized game) once

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
N_{\mathrm{iter}}(\varepsilon,\delta)=T=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\cdot\frac{\log({\rm e}/\delta)}{\varepsilon}}}\right),\qquad N_{\mathrm{sample}}(\varepsilon,\delta)=\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}B_{t}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{T^{2}}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}}}\right).
``` |  |

In particular (since here
$`v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=6D^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\log^{2}(1/\varepsilon))`$),
$`N_{\mathrm{iter}}(\varepsilon)=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\varepsilon^{-1})`$
and
$`N_{\mathrm{sample}}(\varepsilon)=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\varepsilon^{-2})`$
up to polylog factors.

###### Proof.

By
Proposition [5](#Thmproposition5 "Proposition 5 (Softmax parametrization satisfies Assumptions –). ‣ Parametrization. ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
and
Lemma [17](#Thmlemma17 "Lemma 17. ‣ C.3.3 Gradient Estimation and Noise Bounds ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
Assumptions [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Lipschitzness of parametrization). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")–[6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
hold with $`G=1`$, $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}=0`$, and the stated
$`m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$ and $`v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$.
The condition $`\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\geq G^{2}`$ ensures
Proposition [4](#Thmproposition4 "Proposition 4 (Stochastic rates). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
applies. With the chosen $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}`$, the bias term
in
Proposition [4](#Thmproposition4 "Proposition 4 (Stochastic rates). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
satisfies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{6\log({\rm e}/\delta)}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\cdot\frac{15}{7}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2}\leq\varepsilon/2.
``` |  |

Taking
$`T=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\!\big(\frac{v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\cdot\frac{\log({\rm e}/\delta)}{\varepsilon}\big)`$
makes the remaining (decaying) terms in
Proposition [4](#Thmproposition4 "Proposition 4 (Stochastic rates). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
at most $`\varepsilon/2`$, hence
$`\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{T})\leq\varepsilon`$.
Finally,
$`B_{t}=\lceil(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})/m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\rceil`$
implies
$`\sum_{t<T}B_{t}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2}/m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})`$.
∎

#### C.3.1 Lipschitzness and smoothness

###### Lemma 12 (Lipschitz parametrization, Lemma 24 of [Mei et al. 2020](#bib.bib29)).

For any $`\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\Theta`$ it holds

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\pi_{\theta}-\pi_{\theta^{\prime}}\|_{1}\leq\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|_{\mathrm{span}}\leq\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|_{2}.
``` |  |

In particular,
Assumption [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Lipschitzness of parametrization). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
holds with $`G=1`$.

###### Lemma 13 (Smoothness, cf. Lemmas 2 and 14 of [Mei et al. 2020](#bib.bib29)).

The softmax
parametrization ([37](#A3.E37 "In Parametrization. ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
satisfies
Assumption [3](#Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3 (Smoothness). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=\tfrac{5}{2}\bigl(1+\lambda\log(1/\widetilde{\pi}_{\min})\bigr)+\lambda(4+\log|\mathcal{Y}|)\,,
``` |  |

where $`\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}=\min_{y}\widetilde{\pi}(y)`$.

###### Proof.

The result follows from [Mei et al. (2020)](#bib.bib29) by (i)
considering a one-state bandit MDP, (ii) absorbing the cross-entropy
part of the KL term into the reward and rescaling, and (iii) taking the
discount factor $`\gamma=0`$. ∎

###### Lemma 14 (Non-Uniform Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality, Lemma 15 of [Mei et al. 2020](#bib.bib29)).

A function $`J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi)`$ satisfies non-uniform
Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality with a constant
$`c_{\lambda}(\theta)=\lambda\min_{y}\pi_{\theta}^{2}(y)`$, i.e., for
any $`\theta\in\Theta`$ and $`\pi\in\Pi`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi)\|_{2}^{2}\geq 2c_{\lambda}(\theta)(J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi)-J^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}(\pi))\,.
``` |  |

###### Proof.

Follows from Lemma 15 of [Mei et al. (2020)](#bib.bib29) taking
$`S=1,\gamma=0`$. ∎

We note that, according to [Mei et al. (2020)](#bib.bib29), this
parameterization, by default, violates the usual PL-condition
(Assumption [4](#Thmassumption4 "Assumption 4 (Approximate Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
because its coefficient depends on the minimal probability of the
current policy, which can become arbitrarily small during the algorithm
iterates. To address this, we introduce an improvement operator that
enables control over the minimum probability.

#### C.3.2 Improvement Operator and Uniform PL

We use a minimal-probability truncation-style argument, similar to that
of [Zhang et al. (2021)](#bib.bib54) and [Labbi et al.
(2025)](#bib.bib70). Let us fix a policy $`\nu\in\Pi`$ with full support
and a constant $`\tau>0`$. For any policy $`\pi`$ define a ratio w.r.t.
$`\nu`$ as $`r_{\nu}(y)\triangleq\pi(y)/\nu(y)`$, the low-ratio set
$`\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi)\triangleq\{y\in\mathcal{Y}\mid r_{\nu}(y)<\tau\}`$,
and the max-ratio action
$`y_{\max}=\argmax_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}r_{\nu}(y)`$, where ties are
resolved arbitrarily.

Next, we define the improved ratios $`r^{\prime}_{\nu}`$ by

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y)\triangleq\begin{cases}\tau&y\in\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi)\,,\\
r_{\nu}(y)-\frac{1}{\nu(y_{\max})}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi)}\nu(z)(\tau-r_{\nu}(z))&y=y_{\max}\,,\\
r_{\nu}(y)&\text{otherwise}\,.\end{cases}
``` |  | (38) |

and the corresponding policy update
$`\pi^{+}=\mathcal{U}^{\nu}_{\tau}(\pi)`$ via
$`\pi^{+}(y)\triangleq\nu(y)\cdot r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y)`$. It is easy to
verify that $`\pi^{+}`$ defines a correct probability distribution.

###### Lemma 15.

Let

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\tau_{0}\;\triangleq\;\min\Bigl\{\exp\bigl(-\tfrac{1}{\lambda}-2\lVert\log\widetilde{\pi}-\log\nu\rVert_{\mathrm{sp}}\bigr),\;(1+1/\nu_{\min})^{-1}\Bigr\},
``` |  |

where $`\nu_{\min}=\min_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\nu(y)`$. Then for any
$`\tau\in(0,\tau_{0}]`$ and for
$`\pi^{+}=\mathcal{U}^{\nu}_{\tau}(\pi)`$ it holds

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi^{+})\leq\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi)\,,\qquad\forall y\in\mathcal{Y}:\pi^{+}(y)\geq\tau\cdot\nu(y)\,.
``` |  |

###### Proof.

Without loss of generality, we can assume
$`|\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi)|>0`$ since otherwise $`\pi^{+}=\pi`$ and the
second condition is trivially satisfied. Let us define
$`\delta_{\tau}(\pi)=\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi)}\nu(y)\cdot(\tau-r_{\nu}(y))\geq 0`$.
In terms of this accumulated ratio, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lVert\pi-\pi^{+}\rVert_{1}=\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\nu(y)|r_{\nu}(y)-r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y)|=\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi)}\nu(y)\cdot(r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y)-r_{\nu}(y))+\nu(y_{\max})(r_{\nu}(y)-r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y))=2\delta_{\tau}(\pi)\,.
``` |  | (39) |

Then we notice that
$`\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi^{+})\leq\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi)`$
follows from the following inequality for any competitor policy
$`\pi^{c}\in\Pi`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Delta(\pi^{c})\triangleq\mathcal{P}^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi^{+}\succ\pi^{c})-\mathcal{P}^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi\succ\pi^{c})\geq 0\,.
``` |  |

To show that, we apply the following decomposition

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Delta(\pi^{c})=\mathcal{P}^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi^{+}\succ\pi^{c})-\mathcal{P}^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi\succ\pi^{c})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}(\pi^{+}(y)-\pi(y))\mathcal{P}(y\succ\pi^{c})}_{T_{1}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+\lambda\underbrace{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\KL(\pi\|\widetilde{\pi})-\KL(\pi^{+}\|\widetilde{\pi})}}\right]}_{T_{2}}\,.`$ |  |

For the first term, we have the following lower bound

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{1}=\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}(\pi^{+}(y)-\pi(y))\cdot\mathcal{P}(y\succ\pi^{c})\geq-\lVert\pi^{+}-\pi\rVert_{1}\lVert\mathcal{P}(y\succ\pi^{c})\rVert_{\mathrm{sp}}\geq-\delta_{\tau}(\pi)\,,
``` |  |

since $`\pi^{+}-\pi`$ is orthogonal to $`{\bf 1}`$,
$`\lVert\mathcal{P}(y\succ\pi^{c})\rVert_{\mathrm{sp}}\leq 1/2`$, and
([39](#A3.E39 "In Proof. ‣ C.3.2 Improvement Operator and Uniform PL ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
provides an expression for a $`\ell_{1}`$-norm.

For the second term, we first notice that for any policy
$`\pi^{\prime}`$ it holds

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\KL(\pi^{\prime}\|\widetilde{\pi})=\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\pi^{\prime}(y)\log\frac{\pi^{\prime}(y)}{\nu(y)}+\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\pi^{\prime}(y)\log\frac{\nu(y)}{\widetilde{\pi}(y)}=\KL(\pi^{\prime}\|\nu)+\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\pi^{\prime}(y)\log\frac{\nu(y)}{\widetilde{\pi}(y)}\,,
``` |  |

thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{2}=\underbrace{\KL(\pi\|\nu)-\KL(\pi^{+}\|\nu)}_{T_{3}}+\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}(\pi(y)-\pi^{+}(y))\log\frac{\nu(y)}{\widetilde{\pi}(y)}\geq T_{3}-2\delta_{\tau}(\pi)\lVert\log\nu-\log\widetilde{\pi}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp}}\,.
``` |  |

Finally, to analyze $`T_{3}`$, we reformulate KL-divergence as an
$`f`$-divergence for $`f(x)\triangleq x\log x-(x-1)`$ and have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle T_{3}=\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\nu(y)[f(r_{\nu}(y))-f(r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y))]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi)}\nu(y)[f(r_{\nu}(y))-f(r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y))]}_{T_{3,1}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\underbrace{\nu(y_{\max})(f(r_{\nu}(y_{\max}))-f(r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y_{\max})))}_{T_{3,2}}\,.`$ |  |

Due to convexity of $`f`$, we have for any $`u,v\in\mathbb{R}`$:
$`f(u)-f(v)\geq f^{\prime}(v)(u-v)=\log v\cdot(u-v)`$, and thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{3,1}=\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi)}\nu(y)[f(r_{\nu}(y))-f(r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y))]\geq\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi)}\nu(y)f^{\prime}(r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y))(r_{\nu}(y)-r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y))\,.
``` |  |

For all $`y\in\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi)`$ it holds
$`r_{\nu}(y)<r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y)`$ and $`r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y)\geq\tau`$
and, as a result,
$`f^{\prime}(r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y))\geq f^{\prime}(\tau)=\log(\tau)`$.
Thus, for $`\tau\leq 1`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{3,1}\geq\log(1/\tau)\cdot\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi)}\nu(y)(r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y)-r_{\nu}(y))=\log(1/\tau)\cdot\delta_{\tau}(\pi)\,.
``` |  |

To analyze the second term, let us define
$`\nu(\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi))=\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi)}\nu(y)`$.
Then we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle 1=\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\nu(y)r_{\nu}(y)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi)}\nu(y)\underbrace{r_{\nu}(y)}_{\leq\tau}+\sum_{y\not\in\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi)}\nu(y)\underbrace{r_{\nu}(y)}_{\leq r_{\nu}(y_{\max})}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\nu(\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi))\cdot\tau+(1-\nu(\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi)))\cdot r_{\nu}(y_{\max})\,.`$ |  |

As a result, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
r_{\nu}(y_{\max})\geq\frac{1-\nu(\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi))\tau}{1-\nu(\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi))}=1+\frac{\nu(\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi))}{1-\nu(\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi))}\cdot(1-\tau)\geq 1+\nu(\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi))(1-\tau)\,.
``` |  |

At the same time, we have
$`\delta_{\tau}(\pi)\leq\tau\nu(\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi))`$, and thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y_{\max})\geq 1+\nu(\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi))\cdot(1-\tau)-\frac{\delta_{\tau}(\pi)}{\nu(y_{\max})}\geq 1+\nu(\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi))\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{\nu_{\min}}}}\right)}}\right)\,,
``` |  |

Taking $`\tau\leq(1+1/\nu_{\min})^{-1}`$, we have
$`r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y_{\max})\geq 1`$, and, thus, by the same convexity
argument

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{3,2}\geq\nu(y_{\max})\underbrace{\log(r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y_{\max}))}_{\geq 0}\underbrace{(r_{\nu}(y_{\max})-r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y_{\max}))}_{\geq 0}\geq 0\,.
``` |  |

Combining all bounds together, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Delta(\pi^{c})`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+2\lambda\lVert\log\nu-\log\widetilde{\pi}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp}}}}\right)\delta_{\tau}(\pi)+\lambda\log(1/\tau)\cdot\delta_{\tau}(\pi)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\delta_{\tau}(\pi)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lambda\log(1/\tau)-1-2\lambda\lVert\log\nu-\log\widetilde{\pi}\rVert_{\mathrm{sp}}}}\right)\,,`$ |  |

which is non-negative for all

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
0<\tau\leq\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-\frac{1}{\lambda}-2\lVert\log\widetilde{\pi}-\log\nu\rVert_{\mathrm{sp}}}}\right\}.
``` |  |

The second part of the statement for $`y\in\mathcal{Y}_{\tau}(\pi)`$
follows automatically, whereas for $`y_{\max}`$ it follows from a bound
$`r^{\prime}_{\nu}(y_{\max})\geq 1`$. ∎

We then lift this operator to parameter space as an operator
$`\mathcal{T}^{\nu}_{\tau}\colon\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{Y}|}\to\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{Y}|}`$,
defined as

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{T}^{\nu}_{\tau}(\theta)=\log\mathcal{U}^{\nu}_{\tau}(\pi_{\theta})\,.
``` |  |

Alternatively, we can define it (equivalently) as follows

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{T}^{\nu}_{\tau}(\theta)(y)\triangleq\theta(y)+\begin{cases}\log(\tau\cdot\nu(y)/\pi_{\theta}(y))\,,&\pi_{\theta}(y)/\nu(y)<\tau\,,\\
\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\sum_{y^{\prime}}[\nu(y^{\prime})\cdot\tau-\pi_{\theta}(y^{\prime})]_{+}/{\pi_{\theta}(y)}}}\right)&y=\argmax_{y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}}\frac{\pi_{\theta}(y^{\prime})}{\nu(y^{\prime})}\,,\\
0\,,&\text{otherwise}\,.\end{cases}
``` |  | (40) |

where $`[x]_{+}=\max\{x,0\}`$. For this operator, the following lemma is
a straightforward consequence.

###### Lemma 16.

Let

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\tau_{0}\;\triangleq\;\min\Bigl\{\exp\bigl(-\tfrac{1}{\lambda}-2\lVert\log\widetilde{\pi}-\log\nu\rVert_{\mathrm{sp}}\bigr),\;(1+1/\nu_{\min})^{-1}\Bigr\},
``` |  |

where $`\nu_{\min}=\min_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\nu(y)`$. Then for any
$`\tau\in(0,\tau_{0}]`$ and for
$`\theta^{+}=\mathcal{T}^{\nu}_{\tau}(\theta)`$ it holds

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt_{\lambda}^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\pi_{\theta^{+}})\leq\subopt_{\lambda}^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\pi_{\theta})\,,\qquad\forall y\in\mathcal{Y}:\pi_{\theta^{+}}(y)\geq\tau\cdot\nu(y)\,.
``` |  |

In particular,
Assumption [5](#Thmassumption5 "Assumption 5 (Improvement operator). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
holds for $`\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{T}^{\nu}_{\tau_{0}}`$ for any
full-support distribution $`\nu`$.

###### Proof.

Follows directly from a fact that
$`\pi_{\theta^{+}}=\mathcal{U}^{\nu}_{\tau}(\pi_{\theta})`$ and
Lemma [15](#Thmlemma15 "Lemma 15. ‣ C.3.2 Improvement Operator and Uniform PL ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
∎

###### Corollary 3 (Uniform Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality).

Let $`\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{T}^{\nu}_{\tau}`$ be an improvement operator
defined in
Lemma [16](#Thmlemma16 "Lemma 16. ‣ C.3.2 Improvement Operator and Uniform PL ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
For any $`\theta\in\Theta_{\mathcal{T}}`$ the following inequality holds
almost surely

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lVert\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})\rVert_{2}^{2}\geq 2c^{\nu}_{\lambda,\tau}\cdot\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta})\,,
``` |  |

where $`c^{\nu}_{\lambda,\tau}=\lambda\tau^{2}\nu_{\min}^{2}`$. In
particular, for a choice $`\tau=\tau_{0}`$, we have the following
simplified factor

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=\lambda{\rm e}^{-2/\lambda}\cdot c_{\nu}^{2},\qquad c_{\nu}=\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-2\lVert\log\widetilde{\pi}-\log\nu\rVert_{\mathrm{sp}},\log(\nu_{\min}/(1+\nu_{\min}))}}\right\}}}\right)\cdot\nu_{\min}\,,
``` |  |

so
Assumption [4](#Thmassumption4 "Assumption 4 (Approximate Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
holds with $`m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$ and
$`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}=0`$.

###### Proof.

For any $`\theta\in\Theta_{\mathcal{T}}`$,
Lemma [16](#Thmlemma16 "Lemma 16. ‣ C.3.2 Improvement Operator and Uniform PL ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
implies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\pi_{\theta}(y)\geq\tau\nu(y),\qquad\min_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\pi_{\theta}(y)\geq\tau\cdot\nu_{\min}\,.
``` |  |

Thus, by
Lemma [14](#Thmlemma14 "Lemma 14 (Non-Uniform Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality, Lemma 15 of ). ‣ C.3.1 Lipschitzness and smoothness ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lVert\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})\rVert_{2}^{2}\geq 2\lambda\min_{y}\pi^{2}_{\theta}(y)\cdot\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta})\geq 2\lambda\tau^{2}\nu_{\min}^{2}\cdot\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta})\,.
``` |  |

After plugging-in the $`\tau=\tau_{0}`$ we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
c^{\nu}_{\lambda,\tau_{0}}=\lambda\min\{\exp(-2/\lambda)\cdot\exp(-4\lVert\log\widetilde{\pi}-\log\nu\rVert_{\mathrm{sp}}),[\nu_{\min}/(1+\nu_{\min})]^{2}\}\nu_{\min}^{2}\,.
``` |  |

Since $`\exp(-2/\lambda)\leq 1`$, we can simplify the expression and
achieve the following bound

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
c^{\nu}_{\lambda,\tau_{0}}\geq m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=\lambda{\rm e}^{-2/\lambda}\cdot\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-4\lVert\log\widetilde{\pi}-\log\nu\rVert_{\mathrm{sp}},2\log(\nu_{\min}/(1+\nu_{\min}))}}\right\}}}\right)\cdot\nu_{\min}^{2}
``` |  |

∎

#### C.3.3 Gradient Estimation and Noise Bounds

For softmax parametrization, we use the truncated pairwise policy
gradient estimator, discussed in
Appendix [C.2](#A3.SS2 "C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
and in this section we verify the conditions of
Lemma [11](#Thmlemma11 "Lemma 11. ‣ C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
for this parameterization.

###### Lemma 17.

Let us consider the context-free softmax parametrization of policies
$`\pi_{\theta}`$. Then, the mini-batch estimator $`g_{t}`$
in ([36](#A3.E36 "In C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
with the choice $`k=\infty`$ satisfies
Assumption [6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with bias $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}`$, subgaussian constant
$`\sigma^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=D_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}^{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})`$,
and subexponential constant
$`v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=6D_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}^{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})`$,
where

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle D_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}^{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})`$ | $`\displaystyle\triangleq 2\Bigl(1+4\lambda\log\Bigl(\frac{2\sqrt{2}\,\lambda(1+\widetilde{\pi}_{\min})}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}}\Bigr)+4\lambda\log(1/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})\Bigr)^{2}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=2\Bigl(1+4\lambda\log\Bigl(\frac{2\sqrt{2}\,\lambda(1+\widetilde{\pi}_{\min})}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}}\Bigr)\Bigr)^{2},`$ |  |

and $`\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}=\min_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\widetilde{\pi}(y)`$.

###### Proof.

For this lemma, we verify the conditions of
Lemma [11](#Thmlemma11 "Lemma 11. ‣ C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
First of all, we notice that
$`\lVert\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x)\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq 2`$ since for
softmax parameterization we have the following identity
$`[\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y)]_{y^{\prime}}=\mathds{1}\{y=y^{\prime}\}-\pi_{\theta}(y^{\prime})`$.
Next, we see that since $`\widetilde{\pi}(y)>0`$ for any
$`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$, then the second part of
Lemma [11](#Thmlemma11 "Lemma 11. ‣ C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
holds with $`D_{\widetilde{\pi}}=1/\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}`$.

Thus, for any $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}>0`$,
Assumption [6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
is satisfied with a bias bound $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}`$,
subgaussian constant
$`\sigma^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=D^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$
and subexponential constant
$`v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=6D^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$,
where

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
D^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\triangleq 2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+4\lambda\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2\sqrt{2}\cdot\lambda(1+\widetilde{\pi}_{\min})}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}}}}\right)+4\lambda\log(1/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})}}\right)^{2}\,.
``` |  |

∎

### C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization

In this section, we state another set of assumptions under which
Assumptions [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Lipschitzness of parametrization). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")–[6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
hold. In the following, we assume $`\Theta=\mathbb{R}^{d}`$ for some
$`d>0`$. We start from the general assumption on the policy
$`\widetilde{\pi}`$ which depends only on this policy and does not
depend on the parametrization.

###### Assumption 7 (Reference-policy regularity).

Define the per-context minimum mass of the reference policy

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)\triangleq\min_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\widetilde{\pi}(y|x).
``` |  |

Assume that $`\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)>0`$ for $`\rho`$-almost every
$`x`$, and that one of the following moment conditions holds:

- (i)
  Log-moment condition:

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log^{2}\!\Bigl(\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)}\Bigr)}}\right]\leq V_{\widetilde{\pi}}\quad\text{for some }V_{\widetilde{\pi}}>0.
  ``` |  |
- (ii)
  Inverse-mass condition:

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)}}}\right]\leq D_{\widetilde{\pi}}\quad\text{for some }D_{\widetilde{\pi}}>0.
  ``` |  |

This assumption controls how often the reference policy assigns
extremely small probability to some action. It is used to bound moments
(and clipping bias) of log-ratio terms such as
$`\log(\pi_{\theta}(y|x)/\widetilde{\pi}(y|x))`$ that appear in the
KL-regularized policy-gradient estimator.

Condition (ii) is strictly stronger and implies (i). Indeed, for all
$`u\geq 1`$, $`(\log u)^{2}\leq\frac{4}{{\rm e}^{2}}\,u,`$ so taking
$`u=1/\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)`$ yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log^{2}\!\Bigl(\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)}\Bigr)}}\right]\leq\frac{4}{{\rm e}^{2}}\,\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}_{\min}(x)}}}\right]\leq\frac{4}{{\rm e}^{2}}D_{\widetilde{\pi}}.
``` |  |

We state both variants because (i) is sufficient for some bounds (e.g.,
smoothness), whereas (ii) can yield slightly cleaner bias/threshold
choices for clipped estimators.

###### Assumption 8 (Bounded score function).

For all $`\theta\in\Theta`$, the score and the log-policy Hessian are
bounded:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\max_{x\in\mathrm{supp}(\rho),y\in\mathcal{Y}}\|\nabla_{\theta}\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x)\|_{2}^{2}\leq M_{g}^{2},\qquad\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho,y\sim\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\nabla^{2}_{\theta}\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x)\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}}}\right]\leq M_{h}^{2}.
``` |  |

This assumption is standard in literature of convergence of policy
gradient methods (see, e.g., [Papini et al. (2018)](#bib.bib65); [Huang
et al. (2020)](#bib.bib66); [Ding et al. (2022)](#bib.bib62); [Yuan et
al. (2022)](#bib.bib57); [Fatkhullin et al. (2023)](#bib.bib63)).

###### Assumption 9 (Fisher Non-Degeneracy).

The Fisher information matrix is non-degenerate for every
$`\theta\in\Theta_{\mathcal{T}}`$:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
F_{\rho}(\theta)\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho,y\sim\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)}[\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x)[\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x)]^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}]\succeq m_{F}I.
``` |  |

This assumption is natural for any natural policy gradient analysis, and
it holds for certain exponential family parameterized policies ([Ding et
al., 2022](#bib.bib62), Section 8) and for certain neural policy
families. We refer to a discussion in [Liu et al. (2020, Section
B.2)](#bib.bib64).

###### Assumption 10 (Compatible parametrization).

There exists $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bias}}\geq 0`$ such that for
$`u_{\star}(\theta)=F_{\rho}(\theta)^{-1}\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})`$
it holds for any $`\theta\in\Theta_{\mathcal{T}}`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\mathcal{P}(\pi_{\theta}\succ\cdot)+\lambda\log\frac{\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)}{\widetilde{\pi}(\cdot|x)}-u_{\star}(\theta)^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)}}\right\|_{\mathrm{sp}}^{2}}}\right]\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bias}}\,.
``` |  |

We note that this assumption corresponds to an Assumption 4.6 of [Ding
et al. (2022)](#bib.bib62) for $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bias}}=0`$ and
corresponds to the classical assumption of [Sutton et al.
(1999)](#bib.bib67) as well as actively used for analysis of natural
policy gradient methods [Kakade (2001)](#bib.bib69); [Agarwal et al.
(2021)](#bib.bib68). We note that in our case, the norm is more
restrictive than $`\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)`$-weighted $`\ell_{2}`$ norm,
however we argue that even a stronger assumption is possible to satisfy
in practice by using an expressive enough parametrization.

Under these assumptions, we can prove the following result.

###### Proposition 6 (Compatible Fisher non-degenerate parameterization satisfies Assumptions [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Lipschitzness of parametrization). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")–[6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")).

Consider a general parametrization $`\theta\mapsto\pi_{\theta}`$ and an
improvement operator $`\mathcal{T}`$ which satisfy
Assumptions [8](#Thmassumption8 "Assumption 8 (Bounded score function). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
Assumption [9](#Thmassumption9 "Assumption 9 (Fisher Non-Degeneracy). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
and
Assumption [10](#Thmassumption10 "Assumption 10 (Compatible parametrization). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
and assume that a reference policy $`\widetilde{\pi}`$ satisfies
Assumption [7](#Thmassumption7 "Assumption 7 (Reference-policy regularity). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-(i).
Then
Assumption [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Lipschitzness of parametrization). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")–[5](#Thmassumption5 "Assumption 5 (Improvement operator). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
hold with

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
G=M_{g},\quad L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tfrac{1}{2}+\lambda\sqrt{2+V_{\widetilde{\pi}}}}}\right)(M_{g}^{2}+M_{h})+\lambda M_{g}^{2},\quad m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=\frac{\lambda m_{F}^{2}}{2M_{g}^{2}},\quad\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}=\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bias}}\cdot m_{F}^{2}}{M_{g}^{2}}\,.
``` |  |

Additionally, for any $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}>0`$, a truncated
pairwise estimator
([36](#A3.E36 "In C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
with $`k=2`$ satisfies
Assumption [6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with a bias of level $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}`$ and a subgaussian
and subexponential parameters

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=M_{g}^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{8\lambda^{2}M_{g}(V_{\widetilde{\pi}}+1)}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}}}}\right)^{2}\,,\qquad v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=6M_{g}^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{8\lambda^{2}M_{g}(V_{\widetilde{\pi}}+1)}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}}}}\right)^{2}\,.`$ |  |

If additionally a reference policy $`\widetilde{\pi}`$ satisfies
Assumption [7](#Thmassumption7 "Assumption 7 (Reference-policy regularity). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-(ii),
then a truncated pairwise estimator
([36](#A3.E36 "In C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
with $`k=+\infty`$ satisfies
Assumption [6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with a bias level $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}>0`$ and other factors

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\sigma^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=M_{g}^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+4\lambda\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2\lambda\cdot M_{g}(D_{\widetilde{\pi}}+1)}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}}}}\right)}}\right)^{2}\,,\qquad v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=6\sigma^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\,.
``` |  |

###### Proof.

Follows from
Lemma [18](#Thmlemma18 "Lemma 18 (Lipschitzness). ‣ C.4.1 Lipschitzness and smoothness ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
Lemma [19](#Thmlemma19 "Lemma 19 (Smoothness). ‣ C.4.1 Lipschitzness and smoothness ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
Lemma [21](#Thmlemma21 "Lemma 21 (PL inequality). ‣ C.4.2 Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
and
Lemma [22](#Thmlemma22 "Lemma 22. ‣ C.4.3 Gradient Estimation and Noise Bounds ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
∎

###### Corollary 4 (SPG iteration and sample complexity: compatible Fisher parameterization).

Fix $`\delta\in(0,1)`$ and $`\varepsilon\in(0,1)`$. Assume the
log-moment condition of
Assumption [7](#Thmassumption7 "Assumption 7 (Reference-policy regularity). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")(i)
with constant $`V_{\widetilde{\pi}}`$, and assume
Assumptions [8](#Thmassumption8 "Assumption 8 (Bounded score function). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")–[10](#Thmassumption10 "Assumption 10 (Compatible parametrization). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
hold with constants $`M_{g},M_{h},m_{F}`$ and
$`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bias}}`$. Let
$`m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=\lambda m_{F}^{2}/(2M_{g}^{2})`$ and
$`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}=\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bias}}m_{F}^{2}/M_{g}^{2}`$
as in
Proposition [6](#Thmproposition6 "Proposition 6 (Compatible Fisher non-degenerate parameterization satisfies Assumptions –). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
Assume $`\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\geq G^{2}`$ (here
$`G=M_{g}`$), i.e.,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lambda\geq\lambda_{\min}^{\mathrm{F}}\triangleq\sqrt{2}\,\frac{M_{g}^{2}}{m_{F}}.
``` |  |

Run
SPG ([26](#A3.E26 "In Best-response approximation via gradient step. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
with $`(\gamma_{t},B_{t})`$ as in
Proposition [4](#Thmproposition4 "Proposition 4 (Stochastic rates). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
and use the clipped estimator from
Proposition [6](#Thmproposition6 "Proposition 6 (Compatible Fisher non-degenerate parameterization satisfies Assumptions –). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with $`k=2`$ at bias level

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2}=\frac{7\,m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\cdot\varepsilon}{180\log({\rm e}/\delta)}\qquad\text{(so }M=\tfrac{1}{2}+\tfrac{4\lambda^{2}M_{g}(V_{\widetilde{\pi}}+1)}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}}=\Theta(1/\sqrt{\varepsilon})\text{)}.
``` |  |

Then with probability at least $`1-\delta`$, once

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle N_{\mathrm{iter}}(\varepsilon,\delta)=T`$ | $`\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{G^{2}v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\cdot\frac{\log({\rm e}/\delta)}{\varepsilon}}}\right)=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}}}\right),`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle N_{\mathrm{sample}}(\varepsilon,\delta)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}B_{t}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2}/{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}})=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\varepsilon^{-4}),`$ |  |

the final iterate $`\pi_{T}`$ satisfies the approximate VNW guarantee

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{T})\leq\varepsilon+\frac{6\log({\rm e}/\delta)}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}=\varepsilon+\frac{12\log({\rm e}/\delta)}{\lambda}\,\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bias}}.
``` |  |

###### Proof.

Proposition [6](#Thmproposition6 "Proposition 6 (Compatible Fisher non-degenerate parameterization satisfies Assumptions –). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
provides $`G=M_{g}`$, $`m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$,
$`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}`$, and shows
Assumption [6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
holds with
$`v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=6M_{g}^{2}\bigl(1+8\lambda^{2}M_{g}(V_{\widetilde{\pi}}+1)/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}\bigr)^{2}`$.
The condition $`\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\geq G^{2}`$ allows
applying
Proposition [4](#Thmproposition4 "Proposition 4 (Stochastic rates). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
With the chosen $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}`$,
````
