````math
$`\frac{6\log({\rm e}/\delta)}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\cdot\frac{15}{7}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2}\leq\varepsilon/2`$.
Also, since $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2}=\Theta(\varepsilon)`$, we
have $`v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=\Theta(1/\varepsilon)`$, so
taking
$`T=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\!\big(\frac{G^{2}v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}{\lambda m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\cdot\frac{\log({\rm e}/\delta)}{\varepsilon}\big)=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\log({\rm e}/\delta)/\varepsilon^{2})`$
makes the remaining terms in
Proposition [4](#Thmproposition4 "Proposition 4 (Stochastic rates). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
at most $`\varepsilon/2`$, yielding
$`\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{T})\leq\varepsilon+\frac{6\log({\rm e}/\delta)}{m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}`$.
Finally, summing
$`B_{t}=\lceil(t+8\kappa_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})/m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}\rceil`$
gives
$`\sum_{t<T}B_{t}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(T^{2}/m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda})`$.
∎

#### C.4.1 Lipschitzness and smoothness

We note that this type of results for general policy parameterizations
are typically derived for unregularized reinforcement learning problems
([Papini et al., 2018](#bib.bib65); [Yuan et al., 2022](#bib.bib57)). As
a result, it is necessary to prove modified versions.

###### Lemma 18 (Lipschitzness).

Assume
Assumption [8](#Thmassumption8 "Assumption 8 (Bounded score function). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
For any $`\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\Theta`$ it holds

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\pi_{\theta}-\pi_{\theta^{\prime}}\|_{1,\rho}\leq M_{g}\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|_{2}.
``` |  |

In particular,
Assumption [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Lipschitzness of parametrization). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
holds with a constant $`G=M_{g}`$.

###### Proof.

From Lemma 24 of [Mei et al. (2020)](#bib.bib29) we have for any
$`x\in\mathcal{X}`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lVert\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)-\pi_{\theta^{\prime}}(\cdot|x)\rVert_{1}\leq\lVert\log\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)-\log\pi_{\theta^{\prime}}(\cdot|x)\rVert_{\mathrm{sp}}\,.
``` |  |

Next, by Taylor expansion we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\log\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)-\log\pi_{\theta^{\prime}}(\cdot|x)=\nabla\log\pi_{\tilde{\theta}}(\cdot|x)^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}(\theta-\theta^{\prime})\,,
``` |  |

where $`\tilde{\theta}`$ is some point on the line segment between
$`\theta`$ and $`\theta^{\prime}`$. By our assumption on the parameter
set $`\Theta`$ we have $`\tilde{\theta}\in\Theta`$, thus, applying
Assumption [8](#Thmassumption8 "Assumption 8 (Bounded score function). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\|\pi_{\theta}-\pi_{\theta^{\prime}}\|_{1,\rho}^{2}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}[\lVert\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)-\pi_{\theta^{\prime}}(\cdot|x)\rVert_{1}^{2}]\leq\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}[\lVert\nabla\log\pi_{\tilde{\theta}}(\cdot|x)^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}(\theta-\theta^{\prime})\rVert_{\infty}^{2}]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\max_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\lVert\nabla\log\pi_{\tilde{\theta}}(y|x)\rVert_{2}^{2}}}\right]\cdot\lVert\theta-\theta^{\prime}\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq M_{g}^{2}\lVert\theta-\theta^{\prime}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,.`$ |  |

∎

###### Lemma 19 (Smoothness).

Assume
Assumption [8](#Thmassumption8 "Assumption 8 (Bounded score function). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
and
Assumption [7](#Thmassumption7 "Assumption 7 (Reference-policy regularity). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-(i).
Then $`\theta\mapsto J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi)`$ is
$`L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$-smooth on $`\Theta`$ with

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tfrac{1}{2}+\lambda\sqrt{2+V_{\widetilde{\pi}}}}}\right)(M_{g}^{2}+M_{h})+\lambda M_{g}^{2}\,,
``` |  |

i.e., satisfies
Assumption [3](#Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3 (Smoothness). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with a constant $`L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$.

###### Proof.

To simplify the notation, define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
s_{\theta}(x,y)\triangleq\nabla_{\theta}\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x),\qquad H_{\theta}(x,y)\triangleq\nabla_{\theta}^{2}\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x).
``` |  |

Then the standard identities give

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nabla_{\theta}\pi_{\theta}(y|x)=\pi_{\theta}(y|x)\,s_{\theta}(x,y),\qquad\nabla_{\theta}^{2}\pi_{\theta}(y|x)=\pi_{\theta}(y|x)\big(s_{\theta}(x,y)s_{\theta}(x,y)^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}+H_{\theta}(x,y)\big).
``` |  |

Moreover, since $`\sum_{y}\pi_{\theta}(y|x)=1`$, differentiating twice
yields the “Bartlett identity”

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)}\big[s_{\theta}(x,y)s_{\theta}(x,y)^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}+H_{\theta}(x,y)\big]=0,\qquad\forall x\in\mathcal{X},\theta\in\Theta.
``` |  | (41) |

Next, define the per-sample regularized reward

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
r_{\theta}(x,y)\triangleq\mathcal{P}(\pi\succ y\mid x)+\lambda\log\frac{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}\,.
``` |  |

Using $`\nabla_{\theta}\ell_{\theta}(x,y)=s_{\theta}(x,y)`$ and
$`\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\pi_{\theta}}[s_{\theta}]=0`$, one obtains the usual
policy-gradient form

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nabla_{\theta}J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi)=\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)}\Big[s_{\theta}(x,y)\,r_{\theta}(x,y)\Big]\,.
``` |  |

Differentiating once more gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta}^{2}J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)}\Big[r_{\theta}(x,y)\big(s_{\theta}s_{\theta}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}+H_{\theta}\big)\Big]\;+\;\lambda\,\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)}\Big[s_{\theta}s_{\theta}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Big],`$ |  |

where we used
$`\nabla_{\theta}r_{\theta}(x,y)=\lambda s_{\theta}(x,y)`$.

Fix $`x\in\mathcal{X}`$. Because of
([41](#A3.E41 "In Proof. ‣ C.4.1 Lipschitzness and smoothness ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")),
for any scalar $`c(x)`$ we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)}\Big[c(x)\,(s_{\theta}s_{\theta}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}+H_{\theta})\Big]=0.
``` |  |

Therefore we may replace $`r_{\theta}(x,y)`$ by a centered version
without changing the expectation: pick any
$`a(x),b_{\theta}(x)\in\mathbb{R}`$ and write

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
r_{\theta}(x,y)=\big(u_{\pi}(x,y)-a(x)\big)+\lambda\big(\ell_{\theta}(x,y)-b_{\theta}(x)\big)\;+\;\big(a(x)+\lambda b_{\theta}(x)\big),
``` |  |

and the last constant term vanishes when multiplied by
$`(s_{\theta}s_{\theta}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}+H_{\theta})`$
and averaged over $`y`$. Let
$`S_{\theta}(x,y)\triangleq s_{\theta}s_{\theta}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}+H_{\theta}`$.
Now use Cauchy–Schwarz to bound

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Big\|\mathbb{E}_{x,y}\big[r_{\theta}(x,y)S_{\theta}(x,y)\big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{op}}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\Big\|\mathbb{E}_{x,y}\big[(r_{\theta}(x,y)-a(x)-\lambda b_{\theta}(x))\,S_{\theta}(x,y)\big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{op}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{x,y}\big[|r_{\theta}(x,y)-a(x)-\lambda b_{\theta}(x)|\,\|S_{\theta}(x,y)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\big]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{x,y}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(r_{\theta}(x,y)-a(x)-\lambda b_{\theta}(x))^{2}}}\right]\cdot\mathbb{E}_{x,y}[\|S_{\theta}(x,y)\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}]}\,.`$ |  |

For the Hessian term, we have by Minkowski inequality since
$`\|S_{\theta}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|s_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}+\|H_{\theta}\|_{\mathrm{op}}`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{x,y}[\|S_{\theta}(x,y)\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}]}\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{x,y}[\|s_{\theta}(x,y)\|_{2}^{4}]}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{x,y}[\|H_{\theta}(x,y)\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}]}\leq M_{g}^{2}+M_{h}\,.
``` |  |

For the reward variance term, we first apply Minkowski inequality again:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{x,y}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(r_{\theta}(x,y)-a(x)-\lambda b_{\theta}(x))^{2}}}\right]}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{x,y}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(u_{\pi}(x,y)-a(x))^{2}}}\right]}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+\lambda\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{x,y}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(\ell_{\theta}(x,y)-b_{\theta}(x))^{2}}}\right]}\,.`$ |  |

For the first term, since $`u_{\pi}(x,y)\in[0,1]`$, we can pick
$`a(x)=1/2`$ to get

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{x,y}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(u_{\pi}(x,y)-a(x))^{2}}}\right]}\leq\frac{1}{2}.
``` |  |

For the second term, we take $`b_{\theta}(x)=0`$ and decompose as
follows:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x,y}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(\ell_{\theta}(x,y)-b_{\theta}(x))^{2}}}\right]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{x}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\log\frac{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}}}\right)^{2}_{+}}}\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{x}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\log\frac{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}}}\right)^{2}_{+}}}\right]\,.`$ |  |

For the positive part, we use a bound $`\pi_{\theta}(y|x)\leq 1`$ to get

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}_{x}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\log\frac{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}}}\right)^{2}_{+}}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}_{x}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log^{2}\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}}}\right]\leq V_{\widetilde{\pi}},
``` |  |

where the last inequality follows from
Assumption [7](#Thmassumption7 "Assumption 7 (Reference-policy regularity). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-(i).
For the negative part, we use a bound $`\log(x)\leq x-1`$ for any
$`x>0`$ and get the following

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\log\frac{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}}}\right)^{2}_{+}}}\right]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{x}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\log\frac{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}}}\right)^{2}_{+}}}\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{\infty}u\mathbb{P}_{x\sim\rho,y\sim\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log\frac{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}\geq u}}\right]{\rm d}u\,.`$ |  |

To bound the last probability, we notice that for
$`Z=\log\frac{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}`$ we have
$`\mathbb{E}[{\rm e}^{Z}]=1`$, thus, by Markov’s inequality

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{P}_{x\sim\rho,y\sim\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log\frac{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}\geq u}}\right]\leq{\rm e}^{-u}\,,
``` |  |

thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}_{x}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\pi_{\theta}(\cdot|x)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\log\frac{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)}}}\right)^{2}_{+}}}\right]\leq 2\int_{0}^{\infty}u{\rm e}^{-u}{\rm d}u=2\,.
``` |  |

For the remaining term,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Big\|\lambda\,\mathbb{E}_{x,y}[s_{\theta}s_{\theta}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}]\Big\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda\,\mathbb{E}_{x,y}\|s_{\theta}s_{\theta}^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\lambda\,\mathbb{E}_{x,y}\|s_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\lambda M_{g}^{2}.
``` |  |

Combining all the bounds yields the uniform Hessian bound

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\nabla_{\theta}^{2}J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tfrac{1}{2}+\lambda\sqrt{2+V_{\widetilde{\pi}}}}}\right)(M_{g}^{2}+M_{h})+\lambda M_{g}^{2}\,.
``` |  |

A uniform bound on the Hessian implies
$`L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$-smoothness (e.g. by the standard
second-order Taylor remainder bound), proving the lemma. ∎

#### C.4.2 Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality

###### Lemma 20 (Gradient comparison).

Under
Assumptions [8](#Thmassumption8 "Assumption 8 (Bounded score function). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-[9](#Thmassumption9 "Assumption 9 (Fisher Non-Degeneracy). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-[10](#Thmassumption10 "Assumption 10 (Compatible parametrization). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
for any $`\theta\in\Theta`$ the following inequality holds

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lVert\nabla V_{\lambda}^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\pi_{\theta},\pi_{\theta})\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\leq 2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bias}}+\frac{2M_{g}^{2}}{m_{F}^{2}}\lVert\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})\rVert_{2}^{2}\,.
``` |  |

###### Proof.

First, we notice that
$`[\nabla V_{\lambda}^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\pi_{\theta},\pi_{\theta})](x,y)=\mathcal{P}(\pi_{\theta}\succ y\mid x)+\lambda(1+\log\frac{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}{\widetilde{\pi}(y|x)})`$,
thus,
Assumption [10](#Thmassumption10 "Assumption 10 (Compatible parametrization). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
implies
$`\lVert\nabla V_{\lambda}^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\pi_{\theta},\pi_{\theta})-(u_{\star})^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x)\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bias}}`$,
thus, applying triangle inequality and an inequality
$`(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\lVert\nabla V_{\lambda}^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\pi_{\theta},\pi_{\theta})\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bias}}+2\lVert(u_{\star})^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x)\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bias}}+2\lVert(u_{\star})^{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x)\rVert_{\infty,\rho}^{2}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bias}}+2\max_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\,\lVert F_{\rho}(\theta)^{-1}\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})\rVert_{2}^{2}\cdot\lVert\nabla\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x)\rVert_{2,\rho}^{2}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bias}}+\frac{2M_{g}^{2}}{m_{F}^{2}}\lVert\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})\rVert_{2}^{2}\,.`$ |  |

∎

###### Lemma 21 (PL inequality).

Assume
Assumptions [8](#Thmassumption8 "Assumption 8 (Bounded score function). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-[9](#Thmassumption9 "Assumption 9 (Fisher Non-Degeneracy). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-[10](#Thmassumption10 "Assumption 10 (Compatible parametrization). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
Then for any $`\theta\in\Theta_{\mathcal{T}}`$ the following holds

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lambda m_{F}^{2}/M_{g}^{2}\cdot\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta})\leq\lVert\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})\rVert_{2}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bias}}\cdot m_{F}^{2}}{M_{g}^{2}}\,.
``` |  |

In particular,
Assumption [4](#Thmassumption4 "Assumption 4 (Approximate Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
is satisfied with
$`m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=0.5\cdot\lambda(m_{F}/M_{g})^{2}`$ and
$`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}=\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bias}}\cdot(m_{F}/M_{g})^{2}`$ .

###### Proof.

First, we follow a standard proof for PL-inequality in the strongly
convex case, initially context-wise.

Let us fix a context $`x\in\mathrm{supp}(\rho)`$ and a competitor policy
$`\mu\in\Pi`$, and then we can define a context-wise value as
$`V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu|x)=\mathcal{P}(\mu\succ\pi\mid x)+\lambda\KL(\pi(x)\|\widetilde{\pi}(x))`$,
such that
$`V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu)=\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu|x)}}\right]`$.

Let us denote
$`\pi^{\star}_{\mu}(\cdot|x)=\argmin_{\pi}V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu|x)`$
for all $`x\in\mathrm{supp}(\rho)`$ simultaneously. We notice that this
policy is the same as
$`\pi^{\star}_{\mu}=\argmin_{\pi}V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu)`$
due to the additive structure of subproblems with different contexts
$`x`$. Since $`V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu|x)`$ is
$`\lambda`$-strongly convex in $`\pi(x)`$ with respect to
$`\ell_{1}`$-norm, thus, for any $`\pi`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\mu|x)\geq V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu|x)+\langle[\nabla_{\pi}V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu)](x),\pi^{\star}_{\mu}(x)-\pi(x)\rangle+(\lambda/2)\lVert\pi(x)-\pi^{\star}_{\mu}(x)\rVert_{1}^{2}\,.
``` |  |

After rearranging the terms, noticing that
$`\langle{\bf 1},\pi^{\star}_{\mu}(x)-\pi(x)\rangle=0`$ and using a
bound $`ab\leq a^{2}/(2\lambda)+\lambda b^{2}/2`$

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu|x)-V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\mu|x)`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\big\langle[\nabla_{\pi}V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu)](x)+c(x){\bf 1},\pi^{\star}_{\mu}(x)-\pi(x)\big\rangle-(\lambda/2)\lVert\pi(x)-\pi^{\star}_{\mu}(x)\rVert_{1}^{2}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\lambda}\lVert[\nabla_{\pi}V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu)](x)+c(x){\bf 1}\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\,,`$ |  |

where $`c\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{R}`$ is an arbitrary baseline
function. Minimizing the right-hand side over $`c(x)`$ and taking the
expectation over $`x\sim\rho`$ we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu)-V^{\widetilde{\pi},\star}_{\lambda}(\mu)\leq\frac{1}{2\lambda}\lVert\nabla_{\pi}V^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu)\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\,,
``` |  |

and, taking $`\pi=\mu=\pi_{\theta}`$, we get

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta})\leq\frac{1}{2\lambda}\lVert\nabla V_{\lambda}^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\pi_{\theta},\pi_{\theta})\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\,.
``` |  |

Finally, applying
Lemma [20](#Thmlemma20 "Lemma 20 (Gradient comparison). ‣ C.4.2 Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
to derive

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta})\leq\frac{M_{g}^{2}}{\lambda m_{F}^{2}}\|\nabla J^{\widetilde{\pi}}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bias}}}{\lambda}\,.
``` |  |

By rearranging the terms, we conclude the proof. ∎

#### C.4.3 Gradient Estimation and Noise Bounds

For general parametrization, we also use the truncated pairwise policy
gradient estimator, discussed in
Appendix [C.2](#A3.SS2 "C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
Next, we verify the conditions of
Lemma [11](#Thmlemma11 "Lemma 11. ‣ C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
for this parameterization.

###### Lemma 22.

Assume
Assumptions [8](#Thmassumption8 "Assumption 8 (Bounded score function). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
for parameterization and
Assumption [7](#Thmassumption7 "Assumption 7 (Reference-policy regularity). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-(i)
for a reference policy. Then, for any $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}>0`$,
a mini-batch gradient estimator $`g_{t}`$ defined in
([36](#A3.E36 "In C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
with $`k=2`$ satisfies
Assumption [6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with a bias level $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}`$, subgaussian constant
$`\sigma^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=D^{2}_{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})`$,
$`v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=6D_{2}^{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})`$,
where

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
D_{2}^{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})=M_{g}^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{8\lambda^{2}M_{g}(V_{\widetilde{\pi}}+1)}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}}}}\right)^{2}\,.
``` |  |

If, additionally,
Assumption [7](#Thmassumption7 "Assumption 7 (Reference-policy regularity). ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-(ii)
holds, then a mini-batch gradient estimator $`g_{t}`$ defined in
([36](#A3.E36 "In C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
with $`k=+\infty`$ also satisfies
Assumption [6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with a bias level $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}`$, subgaussian constant
$`\sigma^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=D^{2}_{\infty}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})`$,
$`v^{2}_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}=6D^{2}_{\infty}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})`$,
where

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
D_{\infty}^{2}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}})=M_{g}^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+4\lambda\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2\lambda\cdot M_{g}(D_{\widetilde{\pi}}+1)}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}}}}\right)}}\right)^{2}\,.
``` |  |

###### Proof.

Directly follows from
Lemma [11](#Thmlemma11 "Lemma 11. ‣ C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
∎

## Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients

We now instantiate the proximal point (PP) method from
Section [B](#A2 "Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
using the self-play policy gradient (SPG) procedure from
Section [C](#A3 "Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
as an inexact inner solver.

### D.1 Algorithm Description

##### Setup.

Let $`\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}\in\Pi`$ be a reference policy and let
$`\theta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}`$ be initial parameters such that
$`\pi_{0}=\pi_{\theta_{0}}=\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}`$. We fix: (i) the
regularization strength $`\beta>0`$ of the original game, (ii) the
proximal point step size $`\eta>0`$, and (iii) a sequence of self-play
learning rates $`(\gamma_{k,t})_{k\geq 0,\,t\geq 0}`$ and batch sizes
$`(B_{k,t})_{k\geq 0,\,t\geq 0}`$. For convenience, we also denote
$`\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\;\triangleq\;\beta/\eta`$.

The PP update at outer iteration $`k`$ aims to compute

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\pi_{k+1}\approx\argmax_{\pi\in\Pi}\min_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi}\Bigl\{\mathcal{P}(\pi\succ\pi^{\prime})`$ | $`\displaystyle-\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})+\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\prime}\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi_{k})+\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\prime}\|\pi_{k})\Bigr\}.`$ |  |

We realize this update approximately by running self-play policy
gradients (SPG) on the inner two-player game above, using a finite
number $`T_{k}`$ of self-play steps.

##### Inner objective.

At outer iteration $`k`$, we keep $`\pi_{k}`$ fixed and define for any
$`\theta\in\Theta`$ and competitor policy $`\pi\in\Pi`$ the local
objective

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
J_{k}(\theta;\pi)\triangleq\mathcal{P}(\pi\succ\pi_{\theta})+\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{\theta}\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})+\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{\theta}\|\pi_{k}),
``` |  |

where $`\pi_{\theta}`$ is a parametrized policy satisfying
Assumption [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Lipschitzness of parametrization). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")-[5](#Thmassumption5 "Assumption 5 (Improvement operator). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
For a given $`\pi`$, minimizing $`J_{k}(\theta;\pi)`$ in $`\theta`$
corresponds to computing a regularized best response against $`\pi`$
with an additional proximal term toward $`\pi_{k}`$.

##### Stochastic gradients.

At inner iterate $`(k,t)`$ we maintain parameters $`\theta_{k,t}`$ and
the corresponding policy $`\pi_{k,t}=\pi_{\theta_{k,t}}`$. We assume
access to a stochastic gradient estimator $`g_{k,t}`$ which satisfies
Assumption [6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").

##### Example: pairwise REINFORCE estimator.

As a particular instance, we can use a truncated pairwise REINFORCE
estimator, analogous to one defined in
Appendix [C.2](#A3.SS2 "C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
At inner iterate $`(k,t)`$, given a batch size $`B_{k,t}`$, we sample
contexts $`x_{k,t,j}\sim\rho`$, independent pairs
$`(y_{k,t,j},y^{\prime}_{k,t,j})\sim\pi_{k,t}(\cdot\mid x_{k,t,j})\otimes\pi_{k,t}(\cdot\mid x_{k,t,j})`$
and obtain unbiased estimates $`p_{k,t,j}`$ of
$`\mathcal{P}(y_{k,t,j}\succ y^{\prime}_{k,t,j}\mid x_{k,t,j})`$. Define
the single-sample estimator

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle G_{k}(\theta\mid x,y,y^{\prime},p)=\frac{1}{2}\bigl(\nabla_{\theta}\log\pi_{\theta}(y|x)-\nabla_{\theta}\log\pi_{\theta}(y^{\prime}|x)\bigr)\times`$ |  | (42) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\times\mathrm{clip}_{[-M_{k},M_{k}]}\Bigl[\tfrac{1}{2}-p+\beta\bigl(\log\tfrac{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}{\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(y|x)}-\log\tfrac{\pi_{\theta}(y^{\prime}|x)}{\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(y^{\prime}|x)}\bigr)+\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\bigl(\log\tfrac{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}{\pi_{k}(y|x)}-\log\tfrac{\pi_{\theta}(y^{\prime}|x)}{\pi_{k}(y^{\prime}|x)}\bigr)\Bigr],`$ |  |  |

where $`\beta_{\mathrm{target}}=\beta/\eta`$ and $`M_{k}`$ is a clipping
threshold. The mini-batch estimator at step $`(k,t)`$ is

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
g_{k,t}\;\triangleq\;\frac{1}{B_{k,t}}\sum_{j=1}^{B_{k,t}}G_{k}\bigl(\theta_{k,t}\mid x_{k,t,j},y_{k,t,j},y^{\prime}_{k,t,j},p_{k,t,j}\bigr),
``` |  |

which is a (biased) estimate of
$`\nabla J_{k}(\theta_{k,t};\pi_{k,t})`$. As shown in
Appendix [C.2](#A3.SS2 "C.2 Gradient Estimator ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
this choice satisfies
Assumption [6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
under softmax and compatible Fisher non-degenerate parameterization.

##### Self-play update and improvement.

To ensure uniform exploration and obtain a uniform PL constant, we apply
the improvement operator $`\mathcal{T}_{k}`$ for the outer step $`k`$
satisfying
Assumption [5](#Thmassumption5 "Assumption 5 (Improvement operator). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
(e.g., $`\mathcal{T}_{k}\equiv\mathcal{T}^{\nu}_{\tau}`$ from
Section [C.3.2](#A3.SS3.SSS2 "C.3.2 Improvement Operator and Uniform PL ‣ C.3 Verification for the Softmax Parametrization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
in the softmax case). Given $`g_{k,t}`$, the inner update is

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\theta_{k,t+1}\;=\;\mathcal{T}_{k}\bigl(\theta_{k,t}-\gamma_{k,t}\,g_{k,t}\bigr),\qquad\pi_{k,t+1}=\pi_{\theta_{k,t+1}}.
``` |  | (43) |

##### Outer update.

At the end of the inner loop, after $`T_{k}`$ self-play steps, we set

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\theta_{k+1}\;\triangleq\;\theta_{k,T_{k}},\qquad\pi_{k+1}\;\triangleq\;\pi_{k,T_{k}},
``` |  |

and proceed to the next PP iteration. After $`K`$ outer iterations, the
policy $`\pi_{K}`$ is our approximation of the Nash equilibrium of the
original $`\beta`$-regularized preference game. We summarize the
procedure in
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ Outer update. ‣ D.1 Algorithm Description ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").

0:  Reference policy $`\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}`$; regularization parameter
$`\beta>0`$; proximal step size $`\eta>0`$; number of outer iterations
$`K`$; inner iteration lengths $`(T_{k})_{k=0}^{K-1}`$; learning rates
$`(\gamma_{k,t})_{k,t}`$; batch sizes $`(B_{k,t})_{k,t}`$.

0:  Approximate von Neumann winner policy $`\pi_{K}`$.

1:  Initialize parameters $`\theta_{0}`$ such that
$`\pi_{0}=\pi_{\theta_{0}}=\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}`$.

2:  $`\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\leftarrow\beta/\eta`$.

3:  for $`k=0`$ to $`K-1`$ do

4:   \# Outer (proximal point) loop

5:   $`\theta_{k,0}\leftarrow\theta_{k}`$;
 $`\pi_{k,0}\leftarrow\pi_{k}`$.

6:   for $`t=0`$ to $`T_{k}-1`$ do

7:    \# Inner self-play loop

8:    for $`j=1`$ to $`B_{k,t}`$ do

9:     Sample context $`x_{k,t,j}\sim\rho`$.

10:     Sample $`y_{k,t,j}\sim\pi_{k,t}`$ and
$`y^{\prime}_{k,t,j}\sim\pi_{k,t}`$ independently.

11:     Obtain an unbiased estimate $`p_{k,t,j}`$ of
$`\mathcal{P}\bigl(y_{k,t,j}\succ y^{\prime}_{k,t,j}\mid x_{k,t,j}\bigr)`$.

12:     Set
$`G_{k}^{(j)}\leftarrow G_{k}\bigl(\theta_{k,t}\mid x_{k,t,j},y_{k,t,j},y^{\prime}_{k,t,j},p_{k,t,j}\bigr)`$
using
Eq. ([42](#A4.E42 "In Example: pairwise REINFORCE estimator. ‣ D.1 Algorithm Description ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")).

13:    end for

14:
   $`g_{k,t}\leftarrow\frac{1}{B_{k,t}}\sum_{j=1}^{B_{k,t}}G_{k}^{(j)}`$.

15:
   $`\theta_{k,t+1}\leftarrow\mathcal{T}_{k}\bigl(\theta_{k,t}-\gamma_{k,t}\,g_{k,t}\bigr)`$.

16:    $`\pi_{k,t+1}\leftarrow\pi_{\theta_{k,t+1}}`$.

17:   end for

18:   $`\theta_{k+1}\leftarrow\theta_{k,T_{k}}`$;
 $`\pi_{k+1}\leftarrow\pi_{k,T_{k}}`$.

19:  end for

20:  return $`\pi_{K}`$.

Algorithm 1 Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients
(PP–SPG)

### D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG

In this section, we combine the approximate proximal point analysis from
Section [B](#A2 "Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with the self-play policy-gradient results of
Section [C](#A3 "Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
to obtain convergence guarantees for
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ Outer update. ‣ D.1 Algorithm Description ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").

#### D.2.1 Inner PP game as a regularized preference game

Recall that the outer PP iteration (indexed by $`k`$) aims to compute

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\pi_{k+1}\approx\argmax_{\pi\in\Pi}\min_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi}\Bigl\{\mathcal{P}(\pi\succ\pi^{\prime})`$ | $`\displaystyle-\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})+\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\prime}\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi_{k})+\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\prime}\|\pi_{k})\Bigr\}\,,`$ |  |

where $`\beta_{\mathrm{target}}=\beta/\eta`$ and $`\eta>0`$ is the PP
step size. As in
Section [B](#A2 "Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
we focus on the value function of the $`\min`$-player (the regularized
best-response objective)

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{k}(\pi,\mu)\triangleq\mathcal{P}(\mu\succ\pi)+\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})+\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi_{k})\,.
``` |  |

The self-play inner loop at outer iterate $`k`$ is run on the objective

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
J_{k}(\theta;\pi)\;\triangleq\;\mathcal{P}(\pi\succ\pi_{\theta})+\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{\theta}\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})+\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{\theta}\|\pi_{k}),
``` |  |

where $`\pi`$ plays the role of the competitor policy in the self-play
game against $`\pi_{\theta}`$. To analyze the inner loop using the
results from
Section [C](#A3 "Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
we first show that the inner game at outer step $`k`$ can be expressed
as a usual $`\lambda`$-regularized preference game with an appropriate
anchor policy.

###### Lemma 23 (Inner game as $`\lambda`$-regularized preference game).

Let
$`\lambda\triangleq\beta+\beta_{\mathrm{target}}=\beta\Bigl(1+\frac{1}{\eta}\Bigr)`$
and define the geometric mixture

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\forall(x,y)\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}:\widetilde{\pi}_{k}(y|x)`$ | $`\displaystyle\propto[\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(y|x)]^{\beta/\lambda}\,[\pi_{k}(y|x)]^{\beta_{\mathrm{target}}/\lambda}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=[\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(y|x)]^{\eta/(1+\eta)}\,[\pi_{k}(y|x)]^{1/(1+\eta)}.`$ |  |

Then, up to additive constants independent of $`\pi`$ and $`\theta`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle V_{k}(\pi,\mu)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}(\mu\succ\pi)+\lambda\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\widetilde{\pi}_{k})+\text{const}=V^{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}}_{\lambda}(\pi,\mu)+\text{const}\,,`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle J_{k}(\theta;\pi)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}(\pi\succ\pi_{\theta})+\lambda\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{\theta}\|\widetilde{\pi}_{k})+\text{const}=J^{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}}_{\lambda}(\theta;\pi)+\text{const}\,,`$ |  |

where $`J^{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}}_{\lambda}`$ is precisely the
best-response objective from
Section [C](#A3 "Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with regularization parameter $`\lambda`$ and anchor
$`\widetilde{\pi}=\widetilde{\pi}_{k}`$.

###### Proof.

For an arbitrary policy $`\pi`$, we use the identity for any context
$`x\in\mathcal{X}`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\beta\KL(\pi(x)\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(x))+\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\KL(\pi(x)\|\pi_{k}(x))=\lambda\KL(\pi(x)\|\widetilde{\pi}_{k}(x))+\text{const}(\pi^{\mathrm{ref}},\pi_{k},x)\,,
``` |  |

which follows by expanding the KL terms and grouping the
$`\pi`$-dependent and constant parts. Taking the expectation with
respect to $`\rho`$ yields the expression for $`V_{k}`$. Substituting
$`\pi=\pi_{\theta}`$ gives the expression for $`J_{k}`$. ∎

By
Lemma [23](#Thmlemma23 "Lemma 23 (Inner game as 𝜆-regularized preference game). ‣ D.2.1 Inner PP game as a regularized preference game ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
each inner problem at outer iterate $`k`$ is exactly of the form studied
in
Section [C](#A3 "Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
with regularization parameter $`\lambda=\beta+\beta_{\mathrm{target}}`$
and anchor $`\widetilde{\pi}_{k}`$. The only difference is that
$`\widetilde{\pi}_{k}`$ now depends on $`\pi_{k}`$, but $`\lambda`$ is
fixed across outer iterations.

#### D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual

The approximate PP analysis in
Proposition [2](#Thmproposition2 "Proposition 2 (Convergence of approximate PP method). ‣ Equivalence with a mixed-reference value. ‣ B.3 Proximal Point Method ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
requires that the inner solver at outer step $`k`$ returns a policy
$`\pi_{k+1}`$ such that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\bigl\|\nabla_{\pi}V_{k}(\pi_{k+1},\pi_{k+1})\bigr\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\leq\varepsilon,
``` |  | (44) |

for some accuracy parameter $`\varepsilon>0`$, uniformly in $`k`$. We
note that in general, it is possible to construct a sequence of
$`\pi_{k}`$ such that for each step
$`\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{k+1})\to 0`$, but
$`\lVert\nabla_{\pi}V_{k}(\pi_{k+1},\pi_{k+1})\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\not\to 0`$.
Thus, we need one additional assumption on the properties of
parametrization to ensure that an approximate solution to the inner
problem will yield small PP residual.

###### Assumption 11 (Gradient compatibility).

There exists $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{gc}}>0`$ and a constant
$`C_{\mathrm{gc}}>0`$ such that for any $`k\in\mathbb{N}`$, for any
$`\theta\in\Theta_{\mathcal{T}_{k}}`$, the following inequality holds

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lVert\nabla_{\pi}V_{k}(\pi_{\theta},\pi_{\theta})\rVert_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\leq C_{\mathrm{gc}}\cdot\lVert\nabla J_{k}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})\rVert_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{gc}}\,.
``` |  |

This assumption holds under both softmax and Fisher compatible
parameterizations (see, e.g.,
Lemma [20](#Thmlemma20 "Lemma 20 (Gradient comparison). ‣ C.4.2 Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality ‣ C.4 Verification under Compatible Fisher Non-Degenerate parameterization ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")).
Next, we state the required regularity assumption which should be
satisfied for each inner game to guarantee that the self-play policy
gradient convergence is independent of step $`k`$.

###### Assumption 12 (Uniform inner-game regularity).

Fix $`\beta>0`$ and $`\eta>0`$, and let

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lambda\triangleq\beta+\beta_{\mathrm{target}}=\beta\Bigl(1+\frac{1}{\eta}\Bigr),\qquad\beta_{\mathrm{target}}=\beta/\eta,
``` |  |

and let $`\widetilde{\pi}_{k}`$ be the mixed anchor from
Lemma [23](#Thmlemma23 "Lemma 23 (Inner game as 𝜆-regularized preference game). ‣ D.2.1 Inner PP game as a regularized preference game ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
There exist constants

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
G,\;L_{\beta,\eta},\;m_{\beta,\eta},\;\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}},\;\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}},\;\sigma^{2}_{\beta,\eta},\;v^{2}_{\beta,\eta}\;>\;0\,,
``` |  |

independent of $`k`$, and a family of improvement operators
$`(\mathcal{T}_{k})_{k\geq 0}`$ such that for every outer iteration
$`k`$ the inner objective
$`J_{k}(\theta;\pi)=J^{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}}_{\lambda}(\theta;\pi)`$
satisfies
Assumptions [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Lipschitzness of parametrization). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
[3](#Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3 (Smoothness). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
[4](#Thmassumption4 "Assumption 4 (Approximate Polyak–Łojasiewicz inequality). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
and
[5](#Thmassumption5 "Assumption 5 (Improvement operator). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with constants bounded by
$`(G,L_{\beta,\eta},m_{\beta,\eta},\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}})`$, and the
mini-batch gradient estimator $`g_{k,t}`$ satisfies
Assumption [6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with bias $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}`$ and variance proxies
$`(\sigma^{2}_{\beta,\eta},v^{2}_{\beta,\eta})`$. Moreover, a PP
step-size $`\eta`$ satisfies PL-compatibility condition uniformly:

|     |                                                  |     |
|-----|--------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \beta(1+1/\eta)\cdot m_{\beta,\eta}\geq G^{2}\,.
       ```                                               |     |

We note that this assumption does not hold automatically for our
previously chosen examples since many constants, such as
$`L_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda},m_{\widetilde{\pi},\lambda}`$, in
Assumption [3](#Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3 (Smoothness). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")–[6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
depend on the reference policy $`\widetilde{\pi}`$, and our sequence of
policies $`\widetilde{\pi}_{k}`$ might be ill-behaved. However, in
Appendix [D.3](#A4.SS3 "D.3 Assumption Verification ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
we show that this assumption is in fact satisfied thanks to established
convergence of the proximal point method in span-seminorm of
log-probabilities.

###### Lemma 24 (From SPG progress to PP residual).

Fix $`k\geq 0`$. Assume
Assumption [11](#Thmassumption11 "Assumption 11 (Gradient compatibility). ‣ D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with constants $`(C_{\mathrm{gc}},\varepsilon_{\mathrm{gc}})`$. Assume
additionally that $`J_{k}(\cdot;\pi)`$ is $`L_{\beta,\eta}`$-smooth (as
in
Assumption [12](#Thmassumption12 "Assumption 12 (Uniform inner-game regularity). ‣ D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")).
Then for any $`\theta\in\Theta_{\mathcal{T}_{k}}`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\bigl\|\nabla_{\pi}V_{k}(\pi_{\theta},\pi_{\theta})\bigr\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\;\leq\;2C_{\mathrm{gc}}\,L_{\beta,\eta}\cdot\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta})\;+\;\varepsilon_{\mathrm{gc}}.
``` |  |

In particular, if the inner solver returns $`\pi_{k+1}`$ such that
$`\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{k+1})\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}`$,
then the PP residual satisfies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\bigl\|\nabla_{\pi}V_{k}(\pi_{k+1},\pi_{k+1})\bigr\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\;\leq\;\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}\quad\text{with}\quad\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}\triangleq 2C_{\mathrm{gc}}L_{\beta,\eta}\,\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{gc}}.
``` |  |

###### Proof.

Assumption [11](#Thmassumption11 "Assumption 11 (Gradient compatibility). ‣ D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\nabla_{\pi}V_{k}(\pi_{\theta},\pi_{\theta})\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\leq C_{\mathrm{gc}}\,\|\nabla_{\theta}J_{k}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{gc}}.
``` |  |

By $`L_{\beta,\eta}`$-smoothness of
$`\theta\mapsto J_{k}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})`$
(Assumption [3](#Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3 (Smoothness). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")),
we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\nabla_{\theta}J_{k}(\theta;\pi_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2L_{\beta,\eta}\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{\theta})\,,
``` |  |

which allows us to conclude the statement. ∎

Additionally, we provide some regularity conditions on the reference
policy $`\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}`$ that serves as the initial policy for our
algorithm.

###### Lemma 25 (Regularity of reference policy.).

Let $`\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}\in\Pi`$ be a full-support reference policy.
Then it holds for any $`\beta>0`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})\leq\frac{1}{2\beta},\qquad\subopt_{\beta}(\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})\leq\frac{1}{2},\qquad\lVert\log\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}-\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\rVert^{2}_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}\leq\frac{1}{4\beta^{2}}\,.
``` |  |

###### Proof.

By optimality conditions, $`\pi^{\star}_{\beta}`$ can be characterized
as a best response against itself, thus, for any
$`x\sim\mathrm{supp}(\rho)`$ and $`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$ we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y|x)=\frac{1}{\beta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1/2-\mathcal{P}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(x)\succ y|x)}}\right)+\log\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(y|x)+c^{\star}(x)\,,
``` |  |

where $`c^{\star}(x)`$ is a log-normalization constant, which is equal
to

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
c^{\star}(x)=-\log\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(\cdot|x)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\beta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1/2-\mathcal{P}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(x)\succ y|x)}}\right)}}\right)}}\right]\,.
``` |  |

In particular, this expression automatically implies the last bound
since

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lVert\log\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(\cdot|x)-\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(\cdot|x)\rVert_{\mathrm{sp}}\leq\frac{1}{\beta}\lVert 1/2-\mathcal{P}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(x)\succ\cdot|x)\rVert_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2\beta}\,.
``` |  |

For the expression for the KL divergence, we use the log-ratio
expression to write

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})=\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{1}{\beta}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(\cdot|x)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[1/2-\mathcal{P}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(x)\succ y|x)}}\right]+c^{\star}(x)}}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\rho}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[c^{\star}(x)}}\right]\,.
``` |  |

We note that by Jensen’s inequality, it holds

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\exp(c^{\star}(x))`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(\cdot|x)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\beta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1/2-\mathcal{P}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(x)\succ y|x)}}\right)}}\right)}}\right]}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\beta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mathcal{P}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(x)\succ\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(x)|x)}}\right)}}\right)}\leq\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{2\beta}}}\right)\,,`$ |  |

thus
$`\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})\leq 1/(2\beta)`$.
Finally, for the suboptimality, we use the definition:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt_{\beta}(\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})=\max_{\pi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{1}{2}-\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}\succ\pi)}}\right\}\,,
``` |  |

where

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}\succ\pi)=\mathcal{P}(\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}\succ\pi)-\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})+\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})\,.
``` |  |

In particular,
$`\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}\succ\pi)=\mathcal{P}(\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}\succ\pi)+\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})\geq 0`$.
Thus, we have $`\subopt_{\beta}(\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})\leq 1/2`$. ∎

###### Lemma 26 (Regularity of initial policy).

Let $`\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}\in\Pi`$ be a full-support reference policy and
let $`\pi_{k}`$ be the policy at outer iteration $`k`$ of
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ Outer update. ‣ D.1 Algorithm Description ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
Then, for any $`\beta>0`$ and $`\eta>0`$, it holds that the mixed anchor
policy $`\widetilde{\pi}_{k}`$ from
Lemma [23](#Thmlemma23 "Lemma 23 (Inner game as 𝜆-regularized preference game). ‣ D.2.1 Inner PP game as a regularized preference game ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
satisfies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{k})\leq\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{k})\,,
``` |  |

where $`\lambda=\beta+\beta_{\mathrm{target}}`$ with
$`\beta_{\mathrm{target}}=\beta/\eta`$.

###### Proof.

Using a property of $`\widetilde{\pi}_{k}`$ being a geometric mixture,
we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}}_{\lambda}(\pi^{\prime}\succ\pi)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}(\pi^{\prime}\succ\pi)-\lambda\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\prime}\|\widetilde{\pi}_{k})+\lambda\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\widetilde{\pi}_{k})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}(\pi^{\prime}\succ\pi)-\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\prime}\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})+\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\prime}\|\pi_{k})+\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi_{k})\,.`$ |  |

Taking $`\pi^{\prime}=\pi_{k}`$, the expression above gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{k}\succ\pi)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}(\pi_{k}\succ\pi)-\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{k}\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})+\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{k}\|\pi_{k})+\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi_{k})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(\pi_{k}\succ\pi)+\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi_{k})\geq\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(\pi_{k}\succ\pi)\,.`$ |  |

Thus, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{k})\leq\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{k})\,.
``` |  |

∎

Finally, we establish a uniform bound on the suboptimality and
log-probability span-norm of the iterates $`\pi_{k}`$ assuming that each
inner problem is solved up to a fixed accuracy. This result will be
useful to select the number of inner iterations in a consistent manner.

###### Lemma 27 (Uniform regularity of PP iterates).

Assume $`\beta\leq 1`$ and fix $`K\geq 0`$. Assume that for all $`k<K`$
the $`k`$-th proximal subproblem is solved up to accuracy
$`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}>0`$:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\bigl\|\nabla_{\pi}V_{k}(\pi_{k+1},\pi_{k+1})\bigr\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}\,.
``` |  |

Then the iterate $`\pi_{K}`$ satisfies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\log\pi_{K}-\log\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}\leq\frac{\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}}{\beta}\,,\qquad\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{K}\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})\leq\frac{1+2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}}{\beta}\,,
``` |  |

and consequently

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{K})\leq\frac{3}{2}+2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}\,,\qquad\big\|\log\pi_{K}-\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\big\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\leq\frac{(1+\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}})^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\,.
``` |  |

###### Proof.

Let $`\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\triangleq\beta/\eta`$ and recall

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{k}(\pi,\mu)=\mathcal{P}(\mu\succ\pi)+\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})+\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\KL_{\rho}(\pi\|\pi_{k}).
``` |  |

Write $`\zeta_{k+1}\triangleq\nabla_{\pi}V_{k}(\pi_{k+1},\pi_{k+1})`$. A
direct differentiation (same as in
Lemma [4](#Thmlemma4 "Lemma 4 (Strong convexity in 𝜋 with respect to KL_𝜌). ‣ B.2 Regularized Preference and Value ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
gives, for $`\rho`$-a.e. $`x`$ and all $`y`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\zeta_{k+1}(x,y)=\mathcal{P}(\pi_{k+1}(x)\succ y\mid x)+\beta\Bigl(1+\log\frac{\pi_{k+1}(y\mid x)}{\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(y\mid x)}\Bigr)+\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\Bigl(1+\log\frac{\pi_{k+1}(y\mid x)}{\pi_{k}(y\mid x)}\Bigr).
``` |  |

The additive “$`+1`$” terms are constant across $`y`$ and therefore
irrelevant for the span seminorm. Thus, for each $`x`$ we may subtract a
baseline $`c_{k+1}(x)`$ and rewrite as

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{P}(\pi_{k+1}(x)\succ y\mid x)+\beta\log\frac{\pi_{k+1}(y\mid x)}{\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(y\mid x)}+\beta_{\mathrm{target}}\log\frac{\pi_{k+1}(y\mid x)}{\pi_{k}(y\mid x)}=e_{k+1}(x,y),
``` |  |

where $`e_{k+1}(x)\triangleq\zeta_{k+1}(x)-c_{k+1}(x){\bf 1}`$ satisfies
$`\|e_{k+1}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}=\|\zeta_{k+1}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}`$.

Rearranging and using
$`\beta+\beta_{\mathrm{target}}=\beta(1+1/\eta)=\beta(1+\eta)/\eta`$, we
obtain the (approximate) log equation

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\log\pi_{k+1}=\frac{\eta}{1+\eta}\log\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}+\frac{1}{1+\eta}\log\pi_{k}-\frac{\eta}{\beta(1+\eta)}\,\mathcal{P}(\pi_{k+1}\succ\cdot)+\frac{\eta}{\beta(1+\eta)}\,e_{k+1}+\text{(const)}.
``` |  | (45) |

Subtract $`\log\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}`$ and take
$`\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}`$. Using Minkowski and that
$`\|e_{k+1}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}`$,
we get

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\|\log\pi_{k+1}-\log\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1+\eta}\,\|\log\pi_{k}-\log\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}+\frac{\eta}{\beta(1+\eta)}\,\|\mathcal{P}(\pi_{k+1}\succ\cdot)\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\eta}{\beta(1+\eta)}\,\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}.`$ |  |

For each $`x`$, the vector
$`y\mapsto\mathcal{P}(\pi_{k+1}(x)\succ y\mid x)`$ takes values in
$`[0,1]`$, hence
$`\|\mathcal{P}(\pi_{k+1}(x)\succ\cdot\mid x)\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}`$,
and therefore
$`\|\mathcal{P}(\pi_{k+1}\succ\cdot)\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}`$.
Thus, defining
$`D_{k}\triangleq\|\log\pi_{k}-\log\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
D_{k+1}\leq\frac{1}{1+\eta}D_{k}+\frac{\eta}{\beta(1+\eta)}\Bigl(\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}\Bigr).
``` |  |

Since
$`D_{0}=\|\log\pi_{0}-\log\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}=0`$,
unrolling the recursion yields for all $`K`$:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
D_{K}\leq\frac{1}{\beta}\Bigl(\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}\Bigr).
``` |  |

This proves the first bound in the lemma.

Bounding $`\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{K}\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})`$ from span of
log-ratios. Fix $`x`$ and abbreviate $`p=\pi_{K}(\cdot\mid x)`$,
$`q=\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}(\cdot\mid x)`$, and $`r=\log(p/q)`$. Because
$`\sum_{y}q_{y}e^{r_{y}}=1`$, we have $`1\leq e^{\max_{y}r_{y}}`$ and
$`1\geq e^{\min_{y}r_{y}}`$, so $`\max r\geq 0\geq\min r`$ and therefore

|     |                                                      |     |
|-----|------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \max_{y}r_{y}\leq\max r-\min r=2\|r\|_{\mathrm{sp}}.
       ```                                                   |     |

Then

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\KL(p\|q)=\sum_{y}p_{y}r_{y}\leq\max_{y}r_{y}\leq 2\|r\|_{\mathrm{sp}}.
``` |  |

Taking expectation over $`x\sim\rho`$ and using
$`\mathbb{E}\|r(x)\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\leq\|r\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}`$ gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{K}\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})\leq 2\|\log\pi_{K}-\log\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}\leq\frac{1+2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}}{\beta}.
``` |  |

Suboptimality bound. By definition,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{K})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\max_{\mu}\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{2}-\mathcal{P}(\pi_{K}\succ\mu)+\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{K}\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})-\beta\KL_{\rho}(\mu\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}})\Bigr)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta\KL_{\rho}(\pi_{K}\|\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}).`$ |  |

Plugging the KL bound yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{K})\leq\tfrac{1}{2}+\bigl(1+2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}\bigr)=\tfrac{3}{2}+2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}\,.
``` |  |

Span bound to $`\pi^{\star}_{\beta}`$. By the triangle inequality,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\log\pi_{K}-\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}\leq\|\log\pi_{K}-\log\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}+\|\log\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}-\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}.
``` |  |

By
Lemma [25](#Thmlemma25 "Lemma 25 (Regularity of reference policy.). ‣ D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
$`\|\log\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}-\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}\leq\frac{1}{2\beta}`$.
Therefore

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\log\pi_{K}-\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}\leq\frac{\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}}{\beta}+\frac{1}{2\beta}=\frac{1+\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}}{\beta},
``` |  |

and squaring gives the last statement of the lemma. ∎

#### D.2.3 Deterministic PP–SPG

For a warm-up, assume the inner-loop gradients are exact:
$`g_{k,t}=\nabla J_{k}(\theta_{k,t};\pi_{k,t})`$.

###### Corollary 5 (Deterministic PP–SPG).

Assume $`\beta\leq 1`$ and let $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}>0`$. Assume
that parameterization and PP learning rate $`\eta`$ satisfy
Assumptions [11](#Thmassumption11 "Assumption 11 (Gradient compatibility). ‣ D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
and
[12](#Thmassumption12 "Assumption 12 (Uniform inner-game regularity). ‣ D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
Then, the deterministic version of
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ Outer update. ‣ D.1 Algorithm Description ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
using

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{k}\geq\frac{4L_{\beta,\eta}}{m_{\beta,\eta}}\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{3/2+2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}}}}\right)
``` |  |

iterations for each inner loop, where
$`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}\triangleq 2C_{\mathrm{gc}}L_{\beta,\eta}\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}+\frac{3\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}}{2m_{\beta,\eta}}}}\right)+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{gc}}`$,
with a learning rate $`\gamma_{k,t}\equiv\gamma=1/(2L_{\beta,\eta})`$,
for any $`k\geq 0`$ it holds

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|\pi_{k})\leq(1+\eta/2)^{-k}\cdot\frac{1}{2\beta}+\frac{2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}{\beta^{2}}\,,
``` |  |

and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{k})\leq(1+\eta/2)^{-k}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{5}{2\beta^{2}}+\frac{1}{\eta}}}\right)+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{5}{2\beta^{2}}+\frac{1}{\eta}}}\right)\frac{4\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}{\beta}\,,
``` |  |

and, moreover,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\big\|\log\pi_{k}-\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\big\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4\beta^{2}}(1+\eta)^{-k}+\frac{1}{\beta^{3}}\cdot(1+\eta/2)^{-k}+\frac{4\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}{\beta^{4}}\,.
``` |  |

###### Proof.

We want to show that for all $`k\geq 0`$, the following bounds hold:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\bigl\|\nabla_{\pi}V_{k}(\pi_{k+1},\pi_{k+1})\bigr\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}\triangleq 2C_{\mathrm{gc}}L_{\beta,\eta}\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}+\frac{3\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}}{2m_{\beta,\eta}}}}\right)+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{gc}}\,.
``` |  |

Assume now $`k\geq 0`$ and that the statements hold for
$`k^{\prime}<k`$. We will show they hold for $`k`$. By
Lemma [25](#Thmlemma25 "Lemma 25 (Regularity of reference policy.). ‣ D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
for $`k=0`$ and by the induction hypothesis and
Lemma [27](#Thmlemma27 "Lemma 27 (Uniform regularity of PP iterates). ‣ D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
for $`k>0`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{k})\leq\frac{3}{2}+2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}\,.
``` |  |

Applying
Proposition [3](#Thmproposition3 "Proposition 3 (Deterministic rates). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
Lemma [26](#Thmlemma26 "Lemma 26 (Regularity of initial policy). ‣ D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
and a bound $`1+x\leq{\rm e}^{x}`$ to the inner loop at each $`k`$ gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{k+1})`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(1-\frac{m_{\beta,\eta}}{4L_{\beta,\eta}}\Bigr)^{T_{k}}\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{k})+\frac{3\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}}{2m_{\beta,\eta}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(1-\frac{m_{\beta,\eta}}{4L_{\beta,\eta}}\Bigr)^{T_{k}}\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{k})+\frac{3\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}}{2m_{\beta,\eta}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\frac{m_{\beta,\eta}}{4L_{\beta,\eta}}T_{k}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{3}{2}+2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}}}\right)+\frac{3\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}}{2m_{\beta,\eta}}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}+\frac{3\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}}{2m_{\beta,\eta}}\,.`$ |  |

By
Lemma [24](#Thmlemma24 "Lemma 24 (From SPG progress to PP residual). ‣ D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\bigl\|\nabla_{\pi}V_{k}(\pi_{k+1},\pi_{k+1})\bigr\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\leq 2C_{\mathrm{gc}}L_{\beta,\eta}\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}+\frac{3\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}}{2m_{\beta,\eta}}}}\right)+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{gc}}=\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}\,.
``` |  |

Thus
Proposition [2](#Thmproposition2 "Proposition 2 (Convergence of approximate PP method). ‣ Equivalence with a mixed-reference value. ‣ B.3 Proximal Point Method ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
applies to the outer PP loop with
$`\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}`$, yielding the following
corollary. ∎

#### D.2.4 Stochastic PP–SPG

We now analyze the stochastic version of
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ Outer update. ‣ D.1 Algorithm Description ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
where the inner-loop gradients are estimated by mini-batches and satisfy
Assumption [6](#Thmassumption6 "Assumption 6 (Mini-batch gradient noise). ‣ Parametrization assumptions. ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
The proof mirrors the deterministic case, but we additionally track the
confidence parameter and apply a union bound over outer iterations.

###### Corollary 6 (Stochastic PP–SPG).

Assume $`\beta\leq 1`$ and let $`\delta\in(0,1)`$. Assume
Assumptions [11](#Thmassumption11 "Assumption 11 (Gradient compatibility). ‣ D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
and
[12](#Thmassumption12 "Assumption 12 (Uniform inner-game regularity). ‣ D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback").
Fix a target inner accuracy $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}>0`$ and a target
number of outer steps $`K\in\mathbb{N}`$. Define the per-outer failure
probability as

|     |                                                                    |     |
|-----|--------------------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \delta_{k}\triangleq\frac{\delta}{K},\qquad k\in\{0,\dots,K-1\}\,.
       ```                                                                 |     |

and a target PP-residual accuracy as

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}\triangleq 2C_{\mathrm{gc}}L_{\beta,\eta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}+\frac{6\log(K{\rm e}/\delta)}{m_{\beta,\eta}}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}+15/7\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2}}}\right)}}\right)+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{gc}}\,.
``` |  |

Define
$`\kappa_{\beta,\eta}=\frac{L_{\beta,\eta}}{m_{\beta,\eta}}\geq 1`$ and
sequences $`(\gamma_{t})_{t\geq 0}`$ and $`(B_{t})_{t\geq 0}`$ as
follows

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\gamma_{t}=\frac{4t+32\kappa_{\beta,\eta}-2}{m_{\beta,\eta}(t+8\kappa_{\beta,\eta})^{2}}=\Theta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{m_{\beta,\eta}t}}}\right)\,,\qquad B_{t}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lceil\frac{t+8\kappa_{\beta,\eta}}{m_{\beta,\eta}}}}\right\rceil=\Theta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{t}{m_{\beta,\eta}}}}\right)\,.
``` |  |

Then,
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ Outer update. ‣ D.1 Algorithm Description ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
with $`\gamma_{k,t}\equiv\gamma_{t}`$ and $`B_{k,t}\equiv B_{t}`$, and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{k}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{v^{2}_{\beta,\eta}}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}}+\frac{\kappa_{\beta,\eta}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{\beta,\eta}(\sigma^{2}_{\beta,\eta}+v^{2}_{\beta,\eta})}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}}}}}\right)
``` |  |

outputs $`\{\pi_{k}\}_{k\in[0,\ldots,K]}`$ such that with probability at
least $`1-\delta`$ for all $`k=0,\dots,K`$ the following holds:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\KL_{\rho}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\|\pi_{k})`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq(1+\eta/2)^{-k}\cdot\frac{1}{2\beta}+\frac{2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}{\beta^{2}},`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{k})`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq(1+\eta/2)^{-k}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{5}{2\beta^{2}}+\frac{1}{\eta}}}\right)+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{5}{2\beta^{2}}+\frac{1}{\eta}}}\right)\frac{4\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}{\beta},`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\big\|\log\pi_{k}-\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\big\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4\beta^{2}}(1+\eta)^{-k}+\frac{1}{\beta^{3}}(1+\eta/2)^{-k}+\frac{4\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}{\beta^{4}}\,.`$ |  |

###### Proof.

We prove that the PP residual condition holds uniformly for all outer
steps with probability at least $`1-\delta`$, and then apply
Proposition [2](#Thmproposition2 "Proposition 2 (Convergence of approximate PP method). ‣ Equivalence with a mixed-reference value. ‣ B.3 Proximal Point Method ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
exactly as in the deterministic case.

Fix an outer iteration $`k\in\{0,\dots,K-1\}`$. Condition on the history
up to the beginning of the $`k`$-th inner loop (so $`\pi_{k}`$ and hence
$`\widetilde{\pi}_{k}`$ are fixed). By
Assumption [12](#Thmassumption12 "Assumption 12 (Uniform inner-game regularity). ‣ D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"),
the inner objective $`J_{k}`$ satisfies the same regularity constants
$`(L_{\beta,\eta},m_{\beta,\eta},\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}})`$ and the
stochastic gradients satisfy the same noise constants
$`(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}},\sigma^{2}_{\beta,\eta},v^{2}_{\beta,\eta})`$.
Therefore
Proposition [4](#Thmproposition4 "Proposition 4 (Stochastic rates). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
is applicable to the inner loop.

Moreover, as in the deterministic proof, for $`k=0`$
Lemma [25](#Thmlemma25 "Lemma 25 (Regularity of reference policy.). ‣ D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
controls the initial outer iterate, and for $`k>0`$ the induction
hypothesis together with
Lemma [27](#Thmlemma27 "Lemma 27 (Uniform regularity of PP iterates). ‣ D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
gives a uniform bound on $`\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{k})`$ and hence via
Lemma [26](#Thmlemma26 "Lemma 26 (Regularity of initial policy). ‣ D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
a uniform bound on $`\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{k})`$
required to instantiate the SPG bound. Concretely, on the event that the
residual bounds hold for all steps $`k^{\prime}<k`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{k})\leq\subopt_{\beta}(\pi_{k})\leq\frac{3}{2}+2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}.
``` |  |

Now apply
Proposition [4](#Thmproposition4 "Proposition 4 (Stochastic rates). ‣ C.1 Convergence Guarantees ‣ Appendix C Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
to the inner run at step $`k`$ with confidence $`\delta_{k}=\delta/K`$
after $`T_{k}`$ steps and required learning rate and batch size
sequences $`(\gamma_{t})`$ and $`(B_{t})`$. It implies that with
probability at least $`1-\delta_{k}`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{k+1})`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{64\cdot\kappa^{2}_{\beta,\eta}\log(K{\rm e}/\delta)}{(T_{k}+8\kappa_{\beta,\eta}-1)^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{3}{2}+2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}}}\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{24\cdot\kappa_{\beta,\eta}\log(K{\rm e}/\delta)\cdot\log(1+T_{k}/(2\kappa_{\beta,\eta}))}{(T_{k}+8\kappa_{\beta,\eta}-1)^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{675\cdot\sigma^{2}_{\beta,\eta}}{49}+2v^{2}_{\beta,\eta}}}\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{6G^{2}\cdot v^{2}_{\beta,\eta}\log(K{\rm e}/\delta)}{\lambda m_{\beta,\eta}\cdot(T_{k}+8\kappa_{\beta,\eta}-1)}+\frac{6\log(K{\rm e}/\delta)}{m_{\beta,\eta}}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}+15/7\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2}}}\right)\,,`$ |  |

where $`\lambda=\beta(1+1/\eta)`$. To guarantee that all terms with
dependence on $`T_{k}`$ are at most $`\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}`$, it
suffices to choose $`T_{k}`$ such that

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\frac{64\cdot\kappa^{2}_{\beta,\eta}\log(K{\rm e}/\delta)}{(T_{k}+8\kappa_{\beta,\eta}-1)^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{3}{2}+2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}}}\right)\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}}{3}\,,`$ |  | (46) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\frac{24\cdot\kappa_{\beta,\eta}\log(K{\rm e}/\delta)\cdot\log(1+T_{k}/(2\kappa_{\beta,\eta}))}{(T_{k}+8\kappa_{\beta,\eta}-1)^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{675\cdot\sigma^{2}_{\beta,\eta}}{49}+2v^{2}_{\beta,\eta}}}\right)\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}}{3}\,,`$ |  | (47) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\frac{6G^{2}\cdot v^{2}_{\beta,\eta}\log(K{\rm e}/\delta)}{\lambda m_{\beta,\eta}\cdot(T_{k}+8\kappa_{\beta,\eta}-1)}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}/3\,.`$ |  | (48) |

Our choice $`\beta(1+1/\eta)\cdot m_{\beta,\eta}=G^{2}`$ simplifies the
last condition
([48](#A4.E48 "In Proof. ‣ D.2.4 Stochastic PP–SPG ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
to

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{k}\geq\frac{18\cdot v^{2}_{\beta,\eta}\log(K{\rm e}/\delta)}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}}-8\kappa_{\beta,\eta}+1\,,
``` |  |

which asymptotically scales as
$`\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v^{2}_{\beta,\eta}\log(K/\delta)/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}}}\right)`$.
For the first two conditions
([46](#A4.E46 "In Proof. ‣ D.2.4 Stochastic PP–SPG ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
and
([47](#A4.E47 "In Proof. ‣ D.2.4 Stochastic PP–SPG ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")),
it suffices to choose

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle T_{k}\geq\max`$ | $`\displaystyle\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{192\cdot\kappa^{2}_{\beta,\eta}\log(K{\rm e}/\delta)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{3}{2}+2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}}}\right)}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}}},`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad\sqrt{\frac{72\cdot\kappa_{\beta,\eta}\log(K{\rm e}/\delta)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{675\cdot\sigma^{2}_{\beta,\eta}}{49}+2v^{2}_{\beta,\eta}}}\right)\log(1+T_{k}/(2\kappa_{\beta,\eta}))}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}}}\Bigg\}\,.`$ |  |

In particular, the first term scales as
$`\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\kappa_{\beta,\eta}\sqrt{\frac{\log(K/\delta)}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}}}}}\right)`$
and the second term scales as
$`\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{\beta,\eta}(\sigma^{2}_{\beta,\eta}+v^{2}_{\beta,\eta})\log(K/\delta)}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}}}}}\right)`$.

Under this choice, we have shown that with probability at least
$`1-\delta_{k}`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\subopt^{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}}_{\lambda}(\pi_{k+1})\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}+\frac{6\log(K{\rm e}/\delta)}{m_{\beta,\eta}}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}+15/7\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2}}}\right).
``` |  |

On the same event,
Lemma [24](#Thmlemma24 "Lemma 24 (From SPG progress to PP residual). ‣ D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\bigl\|\nabla_{\pi}V_{k}(\pi_{k+1},\pi_{k+1})\bigr\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\leq 2C_{\mathrm{gc}}L_{\beta,\eta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{in}}+\frac{6\log(K{\rm e}/\delta)}{m_{\beta,\eta}}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PL}}+15/7\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grad}}^{2}}}\right)}}\right)+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{gc}}=\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}.
``` |  |

Define the event

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{E}\triangleq\bigcap_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathcal{E}_{k}\,,\qquad\mathcal{E}_{k}\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\bigl\|\nabla_{\pi}V_{k}(\pi_{k+1},\pi_{k+1})\bigr\|_{\mathrm{sp},\rho}^{2}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}}}\right\}\,.
``` |  |

By the per-step success probability
$`\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{k})\geq 1-\delta_{k}`$ and a union bound,
$`\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1-\delta`$. On $`\mathcal{E}`$, the
residual condition
([44](#A4.E44 "In D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback"))
holds uniformly for all outer iterations with
$`\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{\mathrm{PP}}`$. Thus
Proposition [2](#Thmproposition2 "Proposition 2 (Convergence of approximate PP method). ‣ Equivalence with a mixed-reference value. ‣ B.3 Proximal Point Method ‣ Appendix B Analysis of Proximal Point Method ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
applies, giving the three displayed bounds. Finally, since
$`\pi_{0}=\pi^{\mathrm{ref}}`$ we use
Lemma [25](#Thmlemma25 "Lemma 25 (Regularity of reference policy.). ‣ D.2.2 Inner accuracy and PP residual ‣ D.2 Analysis of PP–SPG ‣ Appendix D Proximal Point Method with Self-Play Policy Gradients ‣ Proximal Point Nash Learning from Human Feedback")
to bound initial quantities as in the deterministic case. ∎

### D.3 Assumption Verification

First, we prove a technical lemma that will be useful to verify all
assumptions connected to a non-degeneracy of reference policies.

###### Lemma 28.

Let $`\pi,\mu\in\Pi`$ be two full-support policies and define
$`\pi_{\min}(x)=\min_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\pi(y|x)`$ and
````
